) is a straightforward consequence of the definition in (
5.7
) and (
4.16
) when
t\leq T_{\varepsilon}
. In the case
t>T_{\varepsilon}
, we instead have, also using (
4.16
)

\displaystyle\lvert\partial_{x}g_{1,\varepsilon}(x,t)\rvert t

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{g1e})}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\frac{\mathfrak{g}_{1}|\partial_{x}g_{1}(x,T_{\varepsilon})|}{(T_{\varepsilon}^{2}+\mu_{\varepsilon}^{2})^{\frac{\gamma-2}{2}}}(t^{2}+\mu_{\varepsilon}^{2})^{\frac{\gamma-2}{2}}t

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\mathfrak{g}_{1}h(x)[\bar{G}(x,T_{\varepsilon})]^{\hat{\sigma}}g_{1}(x,T_{\varepsilon})}{(T_{\varepsilon}^{2}+\mu_{\varepsilon}^{2})^{\frac{\gamma-2}{2}}}(t^{2}+\mu_{\varepsilon}^{2})^{\frac{\gamma-2}{2}}t

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{h(x)}{\mathfrak{g}_{1}^{\hat{\sigma}}}[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(x,t)]^{\hat{\sigma}}g_{1,\varepsilon}(x,t)t

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{h(x)}{\mathfrak{g}_{1}^{\hat{\sigma}}(1+\hat{\sigma})}\partial_{t}[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(x,t)]^{1+\hat{\sigma}}

that is, (
5.31
). Finally, the properties in (
5.32
)-(
5.33
), follow directly from the definitions in (
5.7
) and (
5.11
), using (
4.4
) and (
5.12
).

Lemma 5

Under assumptions (
4.1
)-(
4.4
) and
(
4.17
)
, the inequalities

\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\displaystyle g_{3,\varepsilon}(x,t)t\leq c[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(x,t)]^{1+\sigma}\\
\\
\displaystyle\frac{[g_{3,\varepsilon}(x,t)]^{2}}{g_{1,\varepsilon}(x,t)}\leq c[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(x,t)]^{1+2\sigma}\end{array}\right.

(5.37)

hold for all
t\geq T
and for a.e.
x\in\Omega
, where
c\equiv c(n,N,\nu,\gamma,c_{\textnormal{b}})
.

Proof

When
t\in[T,T_{\varepsilon})
, the proof of (
5.37
) follows directly by the definitions (
5.7
), (
5.9
), (
5.11
) and from assumption (
4.17
). Therefore we restrict ourselves to the case
t\geq T_{\varepsilon}
. For this, we set
t_{\varepsilon}:=t/T_{\varepsilon}
, and

\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon}(t):=\frac{\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(x,T_{\varepsilon})}{\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(x,t)}\leq 1

(5.38)

and bound via
\eqref{8}_{1}
and (
5.12
) as follows:

\displaystyle g_{3,\varepsilon}(x,t)t

\displaystyle\leq cg_{3}(x,T_{\varepsilon})T_{\varepsilon}\left(\frac{t^{2}+\mu_{\varepsilon}^{2}}{T_{\varepsilon}^{2}+\mu_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right)^{\frac{\gamma-2}{2}}t_{\varepsilon}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq c[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(x,T_{\varepsilon})]^{1+\sigma}t_{\varepsilon}^{\gamma}\leq c(n,\gamma,c_{\textnormal{b}})[\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon}(t)]^{1+\sigma}t_{\varepsilon}^{\gamma}[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(x,t)]^{1+\sigma}\,.

(5.39)

In order to bound
\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon}(t)
we start observing that if
t_{\varepsilon}\leq 1000
, then (
5.37
)
1
follows using (
5.38
). When
t_{\varepsilon}>1000
, we instead estimate

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon}(t)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{gte})}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\frac{\bar{G}(x,T_{\varepsilon})}{\displaystyle\int_{T_{\varepsilon}}^{t}g_{1,\varepsilon}(x,s)s\,ds+(T^{2}+1)^{\gamma/2}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{g1e})}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\frac{\bar{G}(x,T_{\varepsilon})}{\frac{\mathfrak{g}_{1}g_{1}(x,T_{\varepsilon})}{\gamma(T_{\varepsilon}^{2}+\mu_{\varepsilon}^{2})^{\frac{\gamma-2}{2}}}T_{\varepsilon}^{\gamma}\left[\left(t_{\varepsilon}^{2}+(\mu_{\varepsilon}/T_{\varepsilon})^{2}\right)^{\gamma/2}-\left(1+(\mu_{\varepsilon}/T_{\varepsilon})^{2}\right)^{\gamma/2}\right]}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle t_{\varepsilon}>1000}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\frac{c(\gamma)\bar{G}(x,T_{\varepsilon})(T_{\varepsilon}^{2}+\mu_{\varepsilon}^{2})^{\frac{\gamma-2}{2}}}{g_{1}(x,T_{\varepsilon})T_{\varepsilon}^{\gamma}t_{\varepsilon}^{\gamma}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{wewewe})}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle c(\gamma)\left[\frac{g_{1}(x,T_{\varepsilon})T_{\varepsilon}^{2}(T_{\varepsilon}^{2}+\mu_{\varepsilon}^{2})^{\frac{\gamma-2}{2}}}{g_{1}(x,T_{\varepsilon})T_{\varepsilon}^{\gamma}t_{\varepsilon}^{\gamma}}+\frac{(T^{\gamma}+1)(T_{\varepsilon}^{2}+\mu_{\varepsilon}^{2})^{\frac{\gamma-2}{2}}}{g_{1}(x,T_{\varepsilon})T_{\varepsilon}^{\gamma}t_{\varepsilon}^{\gamma}}\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{00bis}}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\frac{c(\gamma)}{t_{\varepsilon}^{\gamma}}\left[1+\frac{T^{\gamma}+1}{\nu T_{\varepsilon}^{\gamma}}\right]\,.

It follows that

\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon}(t)\leq\frac{c(\nu,\gamma)}{t_{\varepsilon}^{\gamma}}

(5.40)

Inserting this last estimate in (
5.39
), we get

g_{3,\varepsilon}(x,t)t\leq ct_{\varepsilon}^{-\sigma\gamma}[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(x,t)]^{1+\sigma}\leq c[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(x,t)]^{1+\sigma},

(5.41)

where
c\equiv c(n,\nu,\gamma,c_{\textnormal{b}})
and the proof of (
5.37
)
1
follows in the case
t_{\varepsilon}>1000
too. As for (
5.37
)
2
, similarly to (
5.37
)
1
, when
t\in[T,T_{\varepsilon})
the proof follows from (
5.7
), (
5.9
) and assumption
\eqref{8}_{2}
, while for
t\geq T_{\varepsilon}
, using
\eqref{8}_{2}
we have

\displaystyle\frac{[g_{3,\varepsilon}(x,t)]^{2}}{g_{1,\varepsilon}(x,t)}

\displaystyle\leq c\frac{[g_{3}(x,T_{\varepsilon})]^{2}}{g_{1}(x,T_{\varepsilon})}\left(\frac{t^{2}+\mu_{\varepsilon}^{2}}{T_{\varepsilon}^{2}+\mu_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right)^{\frac{\gamma-2}{2}}t_{\varepsilon}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq c(n,N,\gamma)[\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon}(t)]^{1+2\sigma}t_{\varepsilon}^{\gamma}[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(x,t)]^{1+2\sigma}

and (
5.37
)
2
follows using (
5.40
) in the last estimate and again arguing as for (
5.41
).

Lemma 6

Under assumptions (
4.1
)-(
4.4
) and (
4.18
) for some
\sigma\geq 0
,
consider a ball
B\Subset\Omega
with
\texttt{r}(B)\leq 1
, numbers
0<\varepsilon_{1}<\varepsilon_{2}\leq\min\{1,T\}/4
and let
w\in W^{1,\gamma}(B;\mathbb{R}^{N})
. If
\bar{G}(\cdot,\lvert Dw\rvert)\in L^{p}(B)
for some
p>1+\sigma
,
then

\int_{B}\left|F_{\varepsilon_{1}}(x,Dw)-F_{\varepsilon_{2}}(x,Dw)\right|\,dx\leq\texttt{o}(\varepsilon_{2})\int_{B}[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(x,\lvert Dw\rvert)]^{p}\,dx+\texttt{o}(\varepsilon_{2})\,,

(5.42)

holds, where
\texttt{o}(\varepsilon_{2})
denotes a quantity such that
\texttt{o}(\varepsilon_{2})\to 0
as
\varepsilon_{2}\to 0
, and

\int_{B}\left|F(x,Dw)-F_{\varepsilon_{2}}(x,Dw)\right|\,dx\leq\texttt{o}(\varepsilon_{2})\int_{B}[\bar{G}(x,\lvert Dw\rvert)]^{p}\,dx+\texttt{o}(\varepsilon_{2})\,.

(5.43)

Proof

Note that (
5.33
) implies
G_{\varepsilon_{1}}(x,t)\leq c(\gamma)G(x,t)
, and therefore we also have that

\bar{G}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\cdot,\lvert Dw\rvert)\in L^{p}(B)
. Next, we denote

\bar{F}_{\varepsilon}(x,t)=\tilde{F}_{\varepsilon}(x,t)-\varepsilon L_{\gamma,\varepsilon}(t):=\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{a}_{\varepsilon}(x,s)s\,ds

(5.44)

and in the following we always take
x\in B
.
In the case it is
|z|\leq\varepsilon_{2}
, by (
4.4
) and (
5.12
) we easily have

\displaystyle\left|\bar{F}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(x,|z|)-\bar{F}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(x,|z|)\right|\leq\left|\bar{F}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(x,\varepsilon_{2})\right|+\left|\bar{F}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(x,\varepsilon_{2})\right|

\displaystyle\ \leq c\|\tilde{a}(\cdot,1)\|_{L^{\infty}(B)}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon_{2}}(s^{2}+\mu_{\varepsilon_{1}}^{2})^{\frac{\gamma-2}{2}}s\,ds

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+c\|\tilde{a}(\cdot,1)\|_{L^{\infty}(B)}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon_{2}}(s^{2}+\mu_{\varepsilon_{2}}^{2})^{\frac{\gamma-2}{2}}s\,ds\leq\texttt{o}(\varepsilon_{2})\,.

When
\varepsilon_{2}\leq|z|\leq T_{\varepsilon_{2}}
, recalling that
\tilde{a}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(x,|z|)\equiv\tilde{a}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(x,|z|)
, by also using the information in the last display we find

\left|\bar{F}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(x,|z|)-\bar{F}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(x,|z|)\right|\leq\left|\bar{F}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(x,\varepsilon_{2})\right|+\left|\bar{F}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(x,\varepsilon_{2})\right|\leq\texttt{o}(\varepsilon_{2})\,.

(5.45)

Finally, note that (
4.4
) and (
5.12
) imply

\tilde{a}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(x,s)\leq c(\gamma)\tilde{a}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(x,s)\leq c(\gamma)\bar{a}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(x,s)\quad\mbox{for $s>T_{\varepsilon_{2}}$}

(5.46)

therefore, when
|z|>T_{\varepsilon_{2}}
, we get

\displaystyle\left|\bar{F}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(x,|z|)-\bar{F}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(x,|z|)\right|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{triine})}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\left|\bar{F}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(x,T_{\varepsilon_{2}})-\bar{F}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(x,T_{\varepsilon_{2}})\right|

\displaystyle\qquad+c\int_{T_{\varepsilon_{2}}}^{|z|}\tilde{a}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(x,s)s\,ds

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{dec2})}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle c\int_{T_{\varepsilon_{2}}}^{|z|}\bar{a}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(x,s)s\,ds+\texttt{o}(\varepsilon_{2})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{a1}}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle c\int_{T_{\varepsilon_{2}}}^{|z|}g_{2,\varepsilon_{1}}(x,s)s\,ds+\texttt{o}(\varepsilon_{2})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{z9})}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle c\int_{T_{\varepsilon_{2}}}^{|z|}[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(x,s)]^{\sigma}g_{1,\varepsilon_{1}}(x,s)s\,ds+\texttt{o}(\varepsilon_{2})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(x,|z|)]^{\sigma}\int_{T_{\varepsilon_{2}}}^{|z|}g_{1,\varepsilon_{1}}(x,s)s\,ds+\texttt{o}(\varepsilon_{2})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(x,|z|)]^{1+\sigma}+\texttt{o}(\varepsilon_{2})\,,

and we have also used that
s\mapsto\bar{G}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(x,s)
is non-decreasing.
Using the content of the last four displays, and using also Hölder inequality, we get

\displaystyle\int_{B}\left|\bar{F}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(x,|Dw|)-\bar{F}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(x,|Dw|)\right|\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\leq\texttt{o}(\varepsilon_{2})|B|+c\int_{B\cap\{|Dw|>T_{\varepsilon_{2}}\}}[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(x,\lvert Dw\rvert)]^{1+\sigma}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\leq\texttt{o}(\varepsilon_{2})|B|+c|B\cap\{|Dw|>T_{\varepsilon_{2}}\}|^{\frac{p-1-\sigma}{p}}\left(\int_{B}[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(x,\lvert Dw\rvert)]^{p}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1+\sigma}{p}}

for
c\equiv c(\nu,\gamma,p)
. On the other hand, observe that

\displaystyle|B\cap\{|Dw|>T_{\varepsilon_{2}}\}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle T_{\varepsilon_{2}}^{-\gamma p}\int_{B\cap\{|Dw|>T_{\varepsilon_{2}}\}}|Dw|^{\gamma p}\,dx

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{z10a}_{1}}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle c\varepsilon_{2}^{\gamma p}\int_{B}[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(x,\lvert Dw\rvert)]^{p}\,dx\,.

Last two inequalities yield

\displaystyle\int_{B}\left|\bar{F}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(x,|Dw|)-\bar{F}_{\varepsilon_{2}}(x,|Dw|)\right|\,dx

\displaystyle\leq|B|\texttt{o}(\varepsilon_{2})

\displaystyle\quad+c\varepsilon_{2}^{\gamma(p-1-\sigma)}\int_{B}[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(x,\lvert Dw\rvert)]^{p}\,dx,

where
c\equiv c(n,N,\nu,\gamma,c_{\textnormal{a}},c_{\textnormal{b}})
.
By using again (
5.19
)
1
, we get

\displaystyle\int_{B}\left|\varepsilon_{1}L_{\gamma,\varepsilon_{1}}(Dw)-\varepsilon_{2}L_{\gamma,\varepsilon_{2}}(Dw)\right|\,dx

\displaystyle\leq c\varepsilon_{2}\int_{B}(|Dw|^{2}+1)^{\gamma/2}\,dx

\displaystyle\leq c\varepsilon_{2}\int_{B}[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(x,\lvert Dw\rvert)]^{p}\,dx\,.

Combining the content of the last two displays and recalling (
5.4
) and (
5.44
) we arrive at (
5.42
). As for (
5.43
), this follows from (
5.6
)
6
and (
5.42
) letting
\varepsilon_{1}\to 0
. Indeed, note that Fatou’s lemma works for the left-hand one; as for the right-hand side, we use again that
G_{\varepsilon}(x,t)\lesssim G(x,t)
and the second information in (
5.33
), and finally Lebesgue dominated convergence.

6
A priori estimates

In this section we develop basic a priori estimates. These are obtained for solutions to certain elliptic systems associated to the integrands defined in Section
5
, (
5.4
). Unless differently specified, we shall permanently assume that
set
in (
4.20
) is in force; all properties (
4.1
)-(
4.18
) will be therefore available as well.
With
0<\varepsilon<\min\{1,T\}/4
and
\mathcal{B}\Subset\Omega
being a ball such that
\texttt{r}(\mathcal{B})\leq 1
, we consider a weak solution
u\in W^{1,\gamma}(\mathcal{B};\mathbb{R}^{N})
to the system

\ \begin{cases}-\,{\rm div}\,a_{\varepsilon}(x,Du)=f\ \ \mbox{in}\ \mathcal{B}\\
f\in L^{\infty}(\mathcal{B};\mathbb{R}^{N}),\ |f|\leq|\mathfrak{f}|,\ \mbox{where}\ \mathfrak{f}\in L^{n}(\mathcal{B};\mathbb{R}^{N})\,,\end{cases}

(6.1)

with
a_{\varepsilon}\colon\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{N\times n}\to\mathbb{R}^{N\times n}
being defined as in (
5.1
). This setting will be kept for the rest of Section
6
. In particular, all the balls considered in the following will have radius
\leq 1
. Eventually, we shall consider additional restrictions of the type
\texttt{r}(\mathcal{B})\leq R_{*}
, where
R_{*}
will be a (small) threshold radius to be determined as a function of the fixed parameters of the problem, but independently of the solution
u
considered. From (
6.1
), and taking (
5.14
) and (
5.23
) into account, it follows that
u
is a minimizer of the functional

\displaystyle w\mapsto\int_{\mathcal{B}}\left[F_{\varepsilon}(x,Dw)-f\cdot w\right]\,dx=\int_{\mathcal{B}}\left[\tilde{F}_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Dw\rvert)-f\cdot w\right]\,dx\,.

(6.2)

We recall that
\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}(\cdot)
, defined in (
5.1
), is such that
x\mapsto\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}(x,t)\in W^{1,d}(\Omega)
for all
t\geq 0
,
t\mapsto\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}(x,t)\in W_{\textnormal{loc}}^{1,\infty}[0,\infty)\cap C^{1}_{\textnormal{loc}}([0,\infty)\setminus\{\varepsilon,T_{\varepsilon}\})
i.e., it is locally
C^{1}
-regular outside
\{\varepsilon,T_{\varepsilon}\}
and it is such that
\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(x,0)=0
for all
x\in\Omega
. This implies that
x\mapsto a_{\varepsilon}(x,z)\in W^{1,d}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{N\times n})
for all
z\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times n}
and
z\mapsto a_{\varepsilon}(x,z)\in W^{1,\infty}_{\textnormal{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{N\times n};\mathbb{R}^{N\times n})
for all
x\in\Omega
.
Finally, the functions
t\mapsto\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,t)
and
g_{1,\varepsilon}(\cdot,t)
are non-decreasing when
\gamma\geq 2
; this is indeed an easy consequence of assumption (
4.4
)
1
(see display after (
4.5
)). A direct consequence of (
5.15
)-(
5.16
) is

\displaystyle\lvert\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(x,\lvert z\rvert)\rvert\lvert z\rvert\leq g_{2,\varepsilon}(x,\lvert z\rvert)\quad\mbox{for all}\ \ (x,z)\in\Omega\times\{\lvert z\rvert\geq T,\ \lvert z\rvert\not=T_{\varepsilon}\}\,.

(6.3)

Indeed, if
\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(x,\lvert z\rvert)\geq 0
, then (
6.3
) trivially follows from (
5.16
)
2
; otherwise (
5.16
)
1
, and then (
5.15
), give
|\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(x,\lvert z\rvert)|\lvert z\rvert=-\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(x,\lvert z\rvert)\lvert z\rvert\leq-g_{1,\varepsilon}(x,\lvert z\rvert)+\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert z\rvert)\leq\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert z\rvert)\leq g_{2,\varepsilon}(x,\lvert z\rvert)
.
Similarly to (
5.15
), by (
5.3
) and (
5.14
), we have that

\begin{cases}\ \varepsilon\mathfrak{g}_{1}(\lvert z\rvert^{2}+\mu_{\varepsilon}^{2})^{(\gamma-2)/2}\leq\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert z\rvert)\leq\Lambda_{\varepsilon}(\lvert z\rvert^{2}+\mu_{\varepsilon}^{2})^{(\gamma-2)/2}\\
\ |\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(x,\lvert z\rvert)|\leq c_{\varepsilon}(\lvert z\rvert^{2}+\mu_{\varepsilon}^{2})^{(\gamma-3)/2}\end{cases}

(6.4)

holds this time for all
(x,z)\in\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{N\times n}
(must be
|z|\not=\varepsilon,T_{\varepsilon}
for (
6.4
)
2
). From (
5.13
)-(
5.14
) we can apply Proposition
6
from Section
12
, and this yields

\displaystyle Du\in L^{\infty}_{\textnormal{loc}}(\mathcal{B};\mathbb{R}^{N\times n})\quad\mbox{and}\quad u\in W^{2,2}_{\textnormal{loc}}(\mathcal{B};\mathbb{R}^{N})\,.

(6.5)

In turn, this implies that

a_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,Du)=\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,|Du|)Du\in W^{1,2}_{\textnormal{loc}}(\mathcal{B};\mathbb{R}^{N\times n})\,.

(6.6)

This follows for instance from the results in
(
dele
, Theorem 1.5)
, together with the expression (chain rule) of
Da_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,Du)
. It is sufficient to check that
\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,|Du|)\in W^{1,2}_{\textnormal{loc}}(\mathcal{B})
and that the corresponding chain rule applies to
D_{s}(\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,|Du|))
, for every
s\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
. After extending
t\mapsto\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}(x,t)
to
\mathbb{R}
by even reflection, we apply
(
dele
, Theorem 1.5)
to the vector field
B\colon\mathcal{B}\times\mathbb{R}\mapsto\mathbb{R}^{n}
defined by
(B(\cdot))_{j}\equiv(\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}(\cdot)\delta_{sj})
. For this, note that for every
t\in\mathbb{R}
,
x\mapsto\tilde{a}_{\varepsilon}(x,t)\in W^{1,n}(\mathcal{B})
(this is
(
dele
, Theorem 1.5, (i))
), and that
\partial_{x}\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}(\cdot)=\partial_{x}\tilde{a}_{\varepsilon}(\cdot)
is still Carathéodory regular; this follows from the definitions (
5.2
)-(
5.3
) and by the fact that
\partial_{x}\tilde{F}^{\prime}
is Carathéodory regular by assumptions (this is
(
dele
, Theorem 1.5, (ii))
). The crucial point to apply the results from
dele
is that, as described in (
5.6
), the set of non-differentiable points of the (extended) partial function
t\mapsto\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}(x,t)
is contained in
\{-T_{\varepsilon},-\varepsilon,\varepsilon,T_{\varepsilon}\}
for every
x\in\mathcal{B}
, and it is therefore a null set which is independent of
x
(this is
(
dele
, Theorem 1.5, (iii))
). Finally,
(
dele
, Theorem 1.5, (iv))
is verified thanks to (
5.13
)
1
and (
6.4
)
2
. Therefore, by (
6.5
),
(
dele
, Theorem 1.5)
applies and (
6.6
) follows with the corresponding chain rule (see (
6.8
) and the related discussion a few lines below). Note that we have used (
6.5
), that, together with (
5.14
)
4
, also implies
\partial_{x_{s}}a_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,Du)\in L^{n}_{{\rm loc}}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{N\times n})
.

Let us write, with abuse of notation (keep in mind
\partial_{z}a_{\varepsilon}(x,z)\equiv\partial_{zz}F_{\varepsilon}(x,z)
is not defined when
|z|=\varepsilon,T_{\varepsilon}
)

\displaystyle(\partial_{z}a_{\varepsilon}(x,z))^{\alpha\beta}_{ij}=\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert z\rvert)\delta_{ij}\delta_{\alpha\beta}+\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{D}}(|z|)\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(x,\lvert z\rvert)\lvert z\rvert\frac{z^{\alpha}_{i}z^{\beta}_{j}}{\lvert z\rvert^{2}}

(6.7)

for
z\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times n}\setminus\{0\}
, and here we are denoting by
\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{D}}(\cdot)
the indicator function of the set
\mathcal{D}:=\mathbb{R}\setminus\{\varepsilon,T_{\varepsilon}\}
. We explain (
6.7
) as follows.
Recalling that
|Du|\in W^{1,2}_{{\rm loc}}(\Omega)
by (
6.5
)-(
6.6
), we have that

\displaystyle D_{s}[a_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,Du)]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{s}}a_{\varepsilon}(x,Du)+\partial_{z}a_{\varepsilon}(x,Du)DD_{s}u

(6.8)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{s}}\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}(x,|Du|)Du+\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}(x,|Du|)\mathds{I}_{N\times n}DD_{s}u

\displaystyle+\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{D}}(|Du|)\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(x,|Du|)D_{s}|Du|

holds a.e. in
\mathcal{B}
, for every
s\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
. Here we are using Einstein’s convention on repeated indexes and
\mathds{I}_{N\times n}\equiv(\delta_{ij}\delta_{\alpha\beta})
.
Exactly as in the autonomous case, the presence of
\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{D}}(|Du|)
in (
6.7
)-(
6.8
) then accounts for the fact that the term
\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(x,|Du|)D_{s}|Du|
is interpreted as zero at those points where
D|Du|=0
, and in particular, for a.e.
x
such that
|Du(x)|\in\{\varepsilon,T_{\varepsilon}\}
, i.e., where
\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(x,|Du(x)|)
alone does not make sense; see
(
dele
, Theorem 1.5)
. Note that, in particular, from (
6.7
), (
5.13
)
1,2
and (
5.15
), it follows that

g_{1,\varepsilon}(x,|z|)|\xi|^{2}\leq\partial_{z}a_{\varepsilon}(x,z)\xi\cdot\xi\,,\quad|\partial_{z}a_{\varepsilon}(x,z)|\leq g_{2,\varepsilon}(x,|z|)\,,

(6.9)

whenever
|z|\geq T
and
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times n}
.

Similar arguments apply to
G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,|Du|)
, this time using directly
(
dele
, Corollary 1.7)
. Indeed, change/extend
g_{1,\varepsilon}(\cdot)
on
\Omega\times(\infty,T)
as
g_{1,\varepsilon}(x,t):=g_{1,\varepsilon}(x,T)
for
t\leq T
(this is continuous as
g_{1,\varepsilon}
is continuous), and, in
dele
, take
b\colon\mathcal{B}\times\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
defined as
(b)_{j}:=(g_{1,\varepsilon}(x,t)t\delta_{ij})_{j}
for every
i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
, with
w(\cdot)\equiv T
,
u(\cdot)\equiv\max\{|Du|,T\}\in W^{1,2}_{{\rm loc}}(\mathcal{B})\cap L^{\infty}_{{\rm loc}}(\mathcal{B})
(here
u(\cdot)
is the one from
dele
, and not the solution considered in (
6.1
)). Finally, note that
\partial_{x_{i}}g_{1,\varepsilon}\in L^{n}_{{\rm loc}}(\mathcal{B}\times\mathbb{R})
by (
5.31
). Then
(
dele
, Corollary 1.7)
implies

G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,|Du|)\in W^{1,2}_{\textnormal{loc}}(\mathcal{B})

(6.10)

and

\displaystyle D_{i}G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)

\displaystyle=\int_{T}^{\max\{\lvert Du\rvert,T\}}\partial_{x_{i}}g_{1,\varepsilon}(x,t)t\,dt

\displaystyle\qquad+g_{1,\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)D_{i}\max\{|Du|,T\}

holds a.e. in
\mathcal{B}
. In particular, on
\{\lvert Du\rvert>T\}
, we have

\displaystyle D_{i}G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)=g_{1,\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)\lvert Du\rvert D_{i}\lvert Du\rvert+\int_{T}^{\lvert Du\rvert}\partial_{x_{i}}g_{1,\varepsilon}(x,t)t\,dt

\displaystyle\quad=g_{1,\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)\sum_{\alpha=1}^{N}\sum_{s=1}^{n}D_{i}D_{s}u^{\alpha}D_{s}u^{\alpha}+\int_{T}^{\lvert Du\rvert}\partial_{x_{i}}g_{1,\varepsilon}(x,t)t\,dt

(6.11)

for every
i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
, where we have also used that

\displaystyle D_{i}\lvert Du\rvert=\frac{1}{\lvert Du\rvert}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{N}\sum_{s=1}^{n}D_{i}D_{s}u^{\alpha}D_{s}u^{\alpha}\,.

(6.12)

Note also that
D_{i}G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)\equiv 0
a.e. on the complement of
\{\lvert Du\rvert>T\}
.

Lemma 7

For
\lambda\equiv(\lambda_{i})\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
z=(z^{\alpha}_{i})\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times n}
,
1\leq i\leq n
and
1\leq\alpha\leq N
, with
|z|\geq T

\displaystyle\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert z\rvert)\lambda\cdot\lambda+\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{D}}(|z|)\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(x,\lvert z\rvert)\lvert z\rvert\sum_{\alpha=1}^{N}\frac{\lvert\lambda\cdot z^{\alpha}\rvert^{2}}{\lvert z\rvert^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\min\left\{\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert z\rvert),\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert z\rvert)+\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{D}}(|z|)\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(x,\lvert z\rvert)\lvert z\rvert\right\}\lvert\lambda\rvert^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\geq g_{1,\varepsilon}(x,\lvert z\rvert)\lvert\lambda\rvert^{2}

(6.13)

holds for every
x\in\Omega
.

Proof

Indeed, (
6.13
) is trivial by (
5.15
)
if
\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{D}}(|z|)\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(x,\lvert z\rvert)\geq 0
(note that (
5.15
) holds when
|z|=T_{\varepsilon}
too). Otherwise, we can estimate simply
\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(x,\lvert z\rvert)\lvert\lambda\cdot z^{\alpha}\rvert^{2}\geq\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(x,\lvert z\rvert)|z^{\alpha}|^{2}|\lambda|^{2}
for every
\alpha
and then use
\eqref{a2}_{1}
.

6.1
Caccioppoli inequality for powers, when
n>2

Up to Section
6.3
included, we concentrate on the case
n>2
, and for the following we set

m:=\frac{d}{d-2}>1\quad\mbox{and}\quad 1\stackrel{{\scriptstyle n<d}}{{<}}\chi:=\frac{2^{*}}{2m}=\frac{n}{n-2}\frac{d-2}{d}\,.

(6.14)

The main result here is

Proposition 1

Let
u\in W^{1,\gamma}(\mathcal{B};\mathbb{R}^{N})
be a solution to
(
6.1
)
, under assumptions
set
in
(
4.20
)
for
n>2
, and replace
(
4.15
)
1
by

\begin{cases}\sigma+\hat{\sigma}<\frac{s_{*}}{n}-\frac{s_{*}}{d}\\
1\leq s_{*}<\min\left\{2m(1+\sigma+\hat{\sigma}),\frac{2n}{n-2}\right\}\,.\end{cases}

(6.15)

Then, for every
p\in[1,\infty)
, there exists a positive radius
R_{*}\equiv R_{*}(\texttt{data},\mathfrak{f}(\cdot),p)\linebreak\leq 1
such that if
\texttt{r}(\mathcal{B})\leq R_{*}
and
B_{\varsigma}\Subset B_{\varrho}
are concentric balls contained in
\mathcal{B}
, then

\displaystyle\|G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\|_{L^{p}(B_{\varsigma})}\leq\frac{c}{(\varrho-\varsigma)^{\beta_{p}}}\left[\|F_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,Du)\|_{L^{s_{*}}(B_{\varrho})}^{\theta_{p}}+1\right]

(6.16)

holds with
c\equiv c(\texttt{data},\lVert h\rVert_{L^{d}(\mathcal{B})})\geq 1
,
\beta_{p},\theta_{p}\equiv\beta_{p},\theta_{p}(n,d,\sigma,\hat{\sigma},p)>0
.

Needless to say, (
6.15
) is implied by (
4.15
)
1
for
s_{*}=1
. The proof of Proposition
1
will take this and the subsequent Sections
6.2
-
6.3
; in the following, all the balls considered but
\mathcal{B}
, will be concentric with
B_{\varsigma}\Subset B_{\varrho}
from the statement. We recall it is
\texttt{r}(\mathcal{B})\leq 1
; the size of
R_{*}
will be chosen in due course of the proof. Observe that all the foregoing computations, except those involving
f
, still work in the case
n=2
; this case will be treated in Section
6.7
below. To start with the proof of Proposition
1
, by (
6.5
)-(
6.7
) we pass to the differentiated form of system (
6.1
), that is

\displaystyle\sum_{s=1}^{n}\int_{\mathcal{B}}\left[\partial_{z}a_{\varepsilon}(x,Du)DD_{s}u\cdot D\varphi+\partial_{x_{s}}a_{\varepsilon}(x,Du)\cdot D\varphi+f\cdot D_{s}\varphi\right]\,dx=0\,,

(6.17)

which holds for all
\varphi\in W^{1,2}_{0}(\mathcal{B};\mathbb{R}^{N})
with compact support in
\mathcal{B}
, by density. Indeed, note that the terms multiplying
D\varphi
belong to
L^{2}_{{\rm loc}}(\mathcal{B})
and
L^{n}_{{\rm loc}}(\mathcal{B})
; this follows from (
5.13
)
3
and (
6.5
). We now consider concentric balls

B_{\varsigma}\subset B_{\tau_{1}}\Subset B_{\tau_{2}}\subset B_{\varrho}
; in particular it is
\varsigma\leq\tau_{1}<\tau_{2}\leq\varrho\leq 1
.
In (
6.17
) we take
\varphi\equiv\varphi_{s}:=\eta^{2}[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{\kappa+1}D_{s}u
for
s\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
, where
\kappa\geq 0
and
\eta\in C^{1}_{{\rm c}}(B_{\varrho})
satisfies

\mathds{1}_{B_{\tau_{1}}}\leq\eta\leq\mathds{1}_{B_{\tau_{2}}}
and
\lvert D\eta\rvert\lesssim 1/(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})
. Note that (
6.5
) and (
6.10
) imply that
\varphi_{s}\in W^{1,2}_{0}(\mathcal{B};\mathbb{R}^{N})
, has support contained in
B_{\tau_{2}}
and, as such, it is admissible in (
6.17
).
It follows

\displaystyle D\varphi_{s}=

\displaystyle\,\eta^{2}[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{\kappa+1}DD_{s}u

\displaystyle+(\kappa+1)\eta^{2}[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{\kappa}D_{s}u\otimes DG_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)

\displaystyle+2\eta[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{\kappa+1}D_{s}u\otimes D\eta\,.

(6.18)

By (
6.5
) and (
6.10
) it follows that
\varphi\in W^{1,2}_{0}(\mathcal{B};\mathbb{R}^{N})
and it is therefore admissible in (
6.17
).
By the last identity we have

\displaystyle\sum_{s=1}^{n}\int_{\mathcal{B}}\partial_{z}a_{\varepsilon}(x,Du)DD_{s}u\cdot D\varphi_{s}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{s=1}^{n}\int_{\mathcal{B}}\eta^{2}[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{\kappa+1}\partial_{z}a_{\varepsilon}(x,Du)DD_{s}u\cdot DD_{s}u\,dx

\displaystyle\qquad+(\kappa+1)\sum_{s=1}^{n}\int_{\mathcal{B}}\eta^{2}[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{\kappa}\partial_{z}a_{\varepsilon}(x,Du)DD_{s}u

\displaystyle\hskip 147.95433pt\cdot\left(D_{s}u\otimes DG_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)\right)\,dx

\displaystyle\qquad+2\sum_{s=1}^{n}\int_{\mathcal{B}}\eta[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{\kappa+1}\partial_{z}a_{\varepsilon}(x,Du)DD_{s}u\cdot(D_{s}u\otimes D\eta)\,dx

\displaystyle\quad=:\mbox{(I)}_{z}+\mbox{(II)}_{z}+\mbox{(III)}_{z}\,.

(6.19)

Note that in the display above and in the following ones until (
6.25
), as
G_{\varepsilon}(x,t)\equiv 0
for
t\leq T
, all the integrals above actually extend only over
B\cap\{\lvert Du\rvert>T\}
, therefore we can always use (
6
) when computing the derivatives of
G_{\varepsilon}
.
To proceed, we have

\displaystyle\mbox{(I)}_{z}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{inaggiunta})}}{{\geq}}

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{B}}\eta^{2}[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{\kappa+1}g_{1,\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)\lvert D^{2}u\rvert^{2}\,dx=:\mathcal{S}_{1}\,.

We temporarily shorten the notation as follows (recall (
4.12
)
1
):

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\kappa}(Du):=(\kappa+1)[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{\kappa}\frac{\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)}{g_{1,\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)}\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\kappa}^{\prime}(Du):=(\kappa+1)\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{D}}(|Du|)[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{\kappa}\frac{\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)\lvert Du\rvert}{g_{1,\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)}\,.

As all the integrals extend over
\{|Du|>T\}
by (
4.6
), it is
g_{1,\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)>0
.
Recalling (
6.7
)-(
6.12
), we then re-write

\displaystyle\mbox{(II)}_{z}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{B}}\eta^{2}\mathcal{H}_{\kappa}(Du)DG_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)\cdot DG_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)\,dx

\displaystyle\hskip 17.07164pt+\sum_{\alpha=1}^{N}\int_{\mathcal{B}}\eta^{2}\mathcal{H}_{\kappa}^{\prime}(Du)\frac{\left(DG_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)\cdot Du^{\alpha}\right)^{2}}{\lvert Du\rvert^{2}}\,dx

\displaystyle\hskip 17.07164pt-\int_{\mathcal{B}}\eta^{2}\mathcal{H}_{\kappa}(Du)\int_{T}^{\lvert Du\rvert}\partial_{x}g_{1,\varepsilon}(x,t)t\,dt\cdot DG_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)\,dx

\displaystyle\hskip 17.07164pt-\sum_{\alpha=1}^{N}\int_{\mathcal{B}}\eta^{2}\mathcal{H}_{\kappa}^{\prime}(Du)\left(\int_{T}^{\lvert Du\rvert}\partial_{x}g_{1,\varepsilon}(x,t)t\,dt\cdot Du^{\alpha}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 122.34685pt\times\frac{(DG_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)\cdot Du^{\alpha})}{\lvert Du\rvert^{2}}\,dx

\displaystyle=:\mbox{(II)}_{z,1}+\mbox{(II)}_{z,2}+\mbox{(II)}_{z,3}+\mbox{(II)}_{z,4}\,.

(6.20)

We now observe that

\mbox{(II)}_{z,1}+\mbox{(II)}_{z,2}\geq(\kappa+1)\int_{\mathcal{B}}\eta^{2}[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{\kappa}\lvert DG_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)\rvert^{2}\,dx=:\mathcal{S}_{2}\,.

(6.21)

Indeed, this follows from (
6.13
) with
\lambda\equiv DG_{\varepsilon}
and
z\equiv Du
. Then we have, by using (
5.15
), (
5.29
), (
5.31
) and (
6.3
)

\displaystyle\ \ |\mbox{(II)}_{z,3}|+|\mbox{(II)}_{z,4}|\leq c(\kappa+1)\int_{\mathcal{B}}\eta^{2}h(x)[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{\kappa}\frac{g_{2,\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)}{g_{1,\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)}

\displaystyle\hskip 99.58464pt\times\left(\int_{T}^{\lvert Du\rvert}\partial_{t}[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(x,t)]^{1+\hat{\sigma}}\,dt\right)\lvert DG_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)\rvert\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\leq c(\kappa+1)\int_{\mathcal{B}}\eta^{2}h(x)[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{\kappa}[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{1+\sigma+\hat{\sigma}}\lvert DG_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)\rvert\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\leq\bar{\varepsilon}\mathcal{S}_{2}+\frac{c(\kappa+1)}{\bar{\varepsilon}}\int_{\mathcal{B}}\eta^{2}[h(x)]^{2}[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{\kappa}[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{2(1+\sigma+\hat{\sigma})}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\leq\bar{\varepsilon}\mathcal{S}_{2}+\frac{c(\kappa+1)\lVert h\rVert_{L^{d}(\mathcal{B})}^{2}}{\bar{\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\times\left(\int_{\mathcal{B}}\eta^{2m}[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{m\kappa}[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{2m(1+\sigma+\hat{\sigma})}\,dx\right)^{1/m}\,,

where
c\equiv c(n,N,\nu,\gamma,c_{\textnormal{b}})
, and arbitrary
\bar{\varepsilon}\in(0,1)
. Note that in last two lines we have used Young and Hölder’s inequalities.
Similarly to (
6.20
), we also have

\displaystyle\mbox{(III)}_{z}

\displaystyle=2\int_{\mathcal{B}}\frac{\eta G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)\mathcal{H}_{\kappa}(Du)}{\kappa+1}DG_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)\cdot D\eta\,dx

\displaystyle\ \ +2\sum_{\alpha=1}^{N}\int_{\mathcal{B}}\frac{\eta G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)\mathcal{H}_{\kappa}^{\prime}(Du)}{(\kappa+1)|Du|^{2}}(DG_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)\cdot Du^{\alpha})(Du^{\alpha}\cdot D\eta)\,dx

\displaystyle\ \ -2\int_{\mathcal{B}}\frac{\eta G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)\mathcal{H}_{\kappa}(Du)}{\kappa+1}\left(\int_{T}^{\lvert Du\rvert}\partial_{x}g_{1,\varepsilon}(x,t)t\,dt\cdot D\eta\right)\,dx

\displaystyle\ \ -2\sum_{\alpha=1}^{N}\int_{\mathcal{B}}\frac{\eta G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)\mathcal{H}_{\kappa}^{\prime}(Du)}{(\kappa+1)|Du|^{2}}

\displaystyle\ \ \qquad\quad\times\left(\int_{T}^{\lvert Du\rvert}\partial_{x}g_{1,\varepsilon}(x,t)t\,dt\cdot Du^{\alpha}\right)(Du^{\alpha}\cdot D\eta)\,dx

\displaystyle=:\mbox{(III)}_{z,1}+\mbox{(III)}_{z,2}+\mbox{(III)}_{z,3}+\mbox{(III)}_{z,4}\,.

Using again (
5.15
), (
5.29
), (
6.3
) and Young inequality, we have, for every
\bar{\varepsilon}\in(0,1)

\displaystyle|\mbox{(III)}_{z,1}|+|\mbox{(III)}_{z,2}|

\displaystyle\ \leq c\int_{\mathcal{B}}\eta[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{\kappa+1}\frac{g_{2,\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)}{g_{1,\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)}\lvert DG_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)\rvert|D\eta|\,dx

\displaystyle\ \leq c\int_{\mathcal{B}}\eta[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{\kappa+1}[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{\sigma}\lvert DG_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)\rvert|D\eta|\,dx

\displaystyle\ \leq\bar{\varepsilon}\mathcal{S}_{2}+\frac{c}{\bar{\varepsilon}(\kappa+1)}\int_{\mathcal{B}}\lvert D\eta\rvert^{2}[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{\kappa}[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{2(1+\sigma)}\,dx

\displaystyle\ \leq\bar{\varepsilon}\mathcal{S}_{2}+\frac{c}{\bar{\varepsilon}(\kappa+1)}\left(\int_{\mathcal{B}}\lvert D\eta\rvert^{2m}[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{m\kappa}[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{2m(1+\sigma)}\,dx\right)^{1/m}

(recall that
\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}\geq 1
) and, using now also (
5.31
), we get

\displaystyle|\mbox{(III)}_{z,3}|+|\mbox{(III)}_{z,4}|

\displaystyle\ \leq c\int_{\mathcal{B}}\eta h(x)[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{\kappa+1}[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(x,|Du|)]^{1+\sigma+\hat{\sigma}}\lvert D\eta\rvert\,dx

\displaystyle\ \leq c\int_{\mathcal{B}}\eta^{2}[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{\kappa+2}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\ +c\int_{\mathcal{B}}\lvert h(x)\rvert^{2}\lvert D\eta\rvert^{2}[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{\kappa}[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{2(1+\sigma+\hat{\sigma})}\,dx

\displaystyle\ \leq c\int_{\mathcal{B}}\eta^{2}[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{\kappa}[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{2(1+\sigma+\hat{\sigma})}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\ +c\lVert h\rVert^{2}_{L^{d}(\mathcal{B})}\left(\int_{\mathcal{B}}\lvert D\eta\rvert^{2m}[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{m\kappa}[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{2m(1+\sigma+\hat{\sigma})}\,dx\right)^{1/m}

\displaystyle\ \leq c\left(\lVert h\rVert^{2}_{L^{d}(\mathcal{B})}+1\right)

(6.22)

\displaystyle\ \quad\times\left(\int_{\mathcal{B}}(\eta^{2m}+\lvert D\eta\rvert^{2m})[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{m\kappa}[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{2m(1+\sigma+\hat{\sigma})}\,dx\right)^{1/m}

with
c\equiv c(n,N,\nu,\gamma,c_{\textnormal{b}})
. Now we look at the second group of terms stemming from (
6.17
)

\displaystyle\sum_{s=1}^{n}\int_{\mathcal{B}}\partial_{x_{s}}a_{\varepsilon}(x,Du)\cdot D\varphi_{s}\,dx

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{latesta})}}{{=}}\sum_{s=1}^{n}\int_{\mathcal{B}}\eta^{2}[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{\kappa+1}\partial_{x_{s}}a_{\varepsilon}(x,Du)\cdot DD_{s}u\,dx

\displaystyle\ +(\kappa+1)\sum_{s=1}^{n}\int_{\mathcal{B}}\eta^{2}[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{\kappa}\partial_{x_{s}}a_{\varepsilon}(x,Du)\cdot(D_{s}u\otimes DG_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert))\,dx

\displaystyle\ +2\sum_{s=1}^{n}\int_{\mathcal{B}}\eta[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{\kappa+1}\partial_{x_{s}}a_{\varepsilon}(x,Du)\cdot(D_{s}u\otimes D\eta)\,dx

\displaystyle\quad=:\mbox{(I)}_{x}+\mbox{(II)}_{x}+\mbox{(III)}_{x}\,.

From
\eqref{growthd}_{3}
,
\eqref{z11}_{2}
, Hölder and Young inequalities, we obtain, for arbitrary
\bar{\varepsilon}\in(0,1)

\displaystyle\lvert\mbox{(I)}_{x}\rvert\leq\,c\int_{\mathcal{B}}\eta^{2}h(x)[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{\kappa+1}g_{3,\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)\lvert D^{2}u\rvert\,dx

\displaystyle\ \leq\bar{\varepsilon}\mathcal{S}_{1}+\frac{c}{\bar{\varepsilon}}\int_{\mathcal{B}}\eta^{2}[h(x)]^{2}[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{\kappa+1}\frac{[g_{3,\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{2}}{g_{1,\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)}\,dx

\displaystyle\ \leq\bar{\varepsilon}\mathcal{S}_{1}+\frac{c}{\bar{\varepsilon}}\lVert h\rVert^{2}_{L^{d}(\mathcal{B})}\left(\int_{\mathcal{B}}\eta^{2m}[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{m\kappa}[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{2m(1+\sigma)}\,dx\right)^{1/m},

where
c\equiv c(n,N,\nu,\gamma,c_{\textnormal{b}})
. Using this time
\eqref{z11}_{1}
we get

\displaystyle\lvert\mbox{(II)}_{x}\rvert\leq

\displaystyle\,c(\kappa+1)\int_{\mathcal{B}}\eta^{2}h(x)[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{\kappa}g_{3,\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)\lvert Du\rvert\lvert DG_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)\rvert\,dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,\bar{\varepsilon}\mathcal{S}_{2}+\frac{c(\kappa+1)}{\bar{\varepsilon}}\int_{\mathcal{B}}\eta^{2}[h(x)]^{2}[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{\kappa}[g_{3,\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{2}\lvert Du\rvert^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,\bar{\varepsilon}\mathcal{S}_{2}+\frac{c(\kappa+1)}{\bar{\varepsilon}}\lVert h\rVert^{2}_{L^{d}(\mathcal{B})}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\times\left(\int_{\mathcal{B}}\eta^{2m}[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{m\kappa}[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{2m(1+\sigma)}\,dx\right)^{1/m}

with
c\equiv c(n,N,\nu,\gamma,c_{\textnormal{b}})
. Again,
\eqref{z11}_{1}
, Young and Hölder inequalities, as in (
6.22
), give

\displaystyle\lvert\mbox{(III)}_{x}\rvert\leq

\displaystyle\,c\int_{\mathcal{B}}\eta h(x)[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{\kappa+1}g_{3,\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)\lvert Du\rvert\lvert D\eta\rvert\,dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,c\int_{\mathcal{B}}\eta^{2}[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{\kappa+2}\,dx

\displaystyle+c\int_{\mathcal{B}}\lvert D\eta\rvert^{2}[h(x)]^{2}[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{\kappa}[g_{3,\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{2}\lvert Du\rvert^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,c\left(\lVert h\rVert^{2}_{L^{d}(\mathcal{B})}+1\right)

(6.23)

\displaystyle\times\left(\int_{\mathcal{B}}(\eta^{2m}+\lvert D\eta\rvert^{2m})[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{m\kappa}[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{2m(1+\sigma)}\,dx\right)^{1/m}

where
c\equiv c(n,N,\nu,\gamma,c_{\textnormal{b}})
. Finally, we examine the contributions to (
6.17
) coming from the terms featuring
f
:

\displaystyle\sum_{s=1}^{n}\int_{\mathcal{B}}f\cdot D_{s}\varphi_{s}\,dx\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{latesta})}}{{=}}\sum_{s=1}^{n}\int_{\mathcal{B}}\eta^{2}[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{\kappa+1}(f\cdot D_{s}D_{s}u)\,dx

\displaystyle\qquad+(\kappa+1)\sum_{s=1}^{n}\int_{\mathcal{B}}\eta^{2}[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{\kappa}D_{s}G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)(f\cdot D_{s}u)\,dx

\displaystyle\qquad+2\sum_{s=1}^{n}\int_{\mathcal{B}}\eta[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{\kappa+1}D_{s}\eta(f\cdot D_{s}u)\,dx

\displaystyle=:\,\mbox{(I)}_{f}+\mbox{(II)}_{f}+\mbox{(III)}_{f}\,.

Here recall that
n>2
. Using
\eqref{z10a}_{2}
, Hölder and Young inequalities we get

\displaystyle\lvert\mbox{(I)}_{f}\rvert\leq

\displaystyle\,c\int_{\mathcal{B}}\eta^{2}[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{\kappa+1}\lvert f\rvert\lvert D^{2}u\rvert\,dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,\bar{\varepsilon}\mathcal{S}_{1}+\frac{c}{\bar{\varepsilon}}\int_{\mathcal{B}}\eta^{2}[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{\kappa}\lvert f\rvert^{2}[g_{1,\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{-1}G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)\,dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,\bar{\varepsilon}\mathcal{S}_{1}+\frac{c}{\bar{\varepsilon}}\int_{\mathcal{B}}\eta^{2}\lvert f\rvert^{2}[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{\kappa}[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{2/\gamma}\,dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,\bar{\varepsilon}\mathcal{S}_{1}+\frac{c}{\bar{\varepsilon}}\lVert f\rVert_{L^{n}(\mathcal{B})}^{2}\left(\int_{\mathcal{B}}\eta^{2^{*}}[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{2^{*}\kappa/2}[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{2^{*}}\,dx\right)^{2/2^{*}}\,,

with
c\equiv c(n,N,\nu,\gamma)
and
2^{*}
is as in (
2.2
). Moreover, by
\eqref{z10a}_{1}
, we get

\displaystyle\lvert\mbox{(II)}_{f}\rvert\leq

\displaystyle\,c(\kappa+1)\int_{\mathcal{B}}\eta^{2}[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{\kappa}\lvert f\rvert\lvert DG_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)\rvert\lvert Du\rvert\,dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,\bar{\varepsilon}\mathcal{S}_{2}+\frac{c(\kappa+1)}{\bar{\varepsilon}}\int_{\mathcal{B}}\eta^{2}\lvert f\rvert^{2}[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{\kappa}[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{2/\gamma}\,dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,\bar{\varepsilon}\mathcal{S}_{2}+\frac{c(\kappa+1)\lVert f\rVert^{2}_{L^{n}(\mathcal{B})}}{\bar{\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\times\left(\int_{\mathcal{B}}\eta^{2^{*}}[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{2^{*}\kappa/2}[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{2^{*}}\,dx\right)^{2/2^{*}}\,,

for
c\equiv c(n,N,\nu,\gamma)
. Finally, again from
\eqref{z10a}_{1}
we deduce that

\displaystyle\lvert\mbox{(III)}_{f}\rvert\leq c\int_{\mathcal{B}}\eta[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{\kappa+1}\lvert f\rvert\lvert D\eta\rvert\lvert Du\rvert\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\leq c\int_{\mathcal{B}}\lvert D\eta\rvert^{2}[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{\kappa+2}\,dx

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+c\int_{\mathcal{B}}\eta^{2}\lvert f\rvert^{2}[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{\kappa}[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{2/\gamma}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\leq c\left(\int_{\mathcal{B}}\lvert D\eta\rvert^{2m}[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{m\kappa}[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{2m}\,dx\right)^{1/m}

\displaystyle\qquad\ +c\lVert f\rVert_{L^{n}(\mathcal{B})}^{2}\left(\int_{\mathcal{B}}\eta^{2^{*}}[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{2^{*}\kappa/2}[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{2^{*}}\,dx\right)^{2/2^{*}}

(6.24)

where
c\equiv c(n,N,\nu,\gamma)
. In the previous three displays, we also used that, by
\eqref{gte}_{2}
it is
\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(\cdot)\geq 1
for all
(x,t)\in\Omega\times[0,\infty)
, thus, for instance
[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(\cdot)]^{1/\gamma}\leq\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(\cdot)
.

Merging the content of displays from (
6.19
) to (
6.24
), with (
6.17
), choosing
\bar{\varepsilon}>0
small enough (in order to reabsorb terms in the usual way), we get, after a few standard manipulations, and again using that
\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(\cdot)\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{1}+\mathcal{S}_{2}\leq c(\kappa+1)\left(\lVert h\rVert_{L^{d}(\mathcal{B})}^{2}+1\right)

(6.25)

\displaystyle\quad\times\left(\int_{\mathcal{B}}(\eta^{2m}+\lvert D\eta\rvert^{2m})[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{m\kappa}[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{2m(1+\sigma+\hat{\sigma})}\,dx\right)^{1/m}

\displaystyle\qquad+c(\kappa+1)\lVert f\rVert_{L^{n}(\mathcal{B})}^{2}\left(\int_{\mathcal{B}}\eta^{2^{*}}[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{2^{*}\kappa/2}[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{2^{*}}\,dx\right)^{2/2^{*}}\,,

with
c\equiv c(n,N,\nu,\gamma,c_{\textnormal{b}})
. Now we note that, by
\eqref{gte}_{2}
it follows

\displaystyle G_{\varepsilon}(x,t)\leq\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(x,t)\leq(T^{2}+1)^{\gamma/2}\left[G_{\varepsilon}(x,t)+1\right]\,,

(6.26)

for all
(x,t)\in\Omega\times[0,\infty)
, therefore, recalling (
6.21
), estimate (
6.25
) can be rearranged as

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{B}}\eta^{2}[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{\kappa}\lvert DG_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)\rvert^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\lVert h\rVert_{L^{d}(\mathcal{B})}^{2}+1\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\left(\int_{\mathcal{B}}(\eta^{2m}+\lvert D\eta\rvert^{2m})\left[[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{m(\kappa+2+2\sigma+2\hat{\sigma})}+1\right]\,dx\right)^{1/m}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ +c\lVert f\rVert_{L^{n}(\mathcal{B})}^{2}\left(\int_{\mathcal{B}}\eta^{2^{*}}\left[[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{\frac{2^{*}}{2}(\kappa+2)}+1\right]\,dx\right)^{2/2^{*}}\,,

where
c\equiv c(n,N,\nu,\gamma,T,c_{\textnormal{b}})
and every number
\kappa\geq 0
. Note that this is the first time a dependence of the constants on
T
appears, via (
6.26
); no blow-up occurs when
T\to 0
, compare with Remark
5
. Sobolev-Poincaré inequality and the previous display give

\displaystyle\left(\int_{\mathcal{B}}\eta^{2^{*}}\left[[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{\frac{2^{*}}{2}(\kappa+2)}+1\right]\,dx\right)^{2/2^{*}}

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054pt\leq\left(\int_{\mathcal{B}}\eta^{2^{*}}\left[[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{\frac{\kappa}{2}+1}+1\right]^{2^{*}}\,dx\right)^{2/2^{*}}

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054pt\leq c\int_{\mathcal{B}}\lvert D(\eta([G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{\frac{\kappa}{2}+1}+1))\rvert^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054pt\leq c(\kappa+1)^{2}\left(\lVert h\rVert_{L^{d}(\mathcal{B})}^{2}+1\right)

(6.27)

\displaystyle\quad\times\left(\int_{\mathcal{B}}(\eta^{2m}+\lvert D\eta\rvert^{2m})\left[[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{m(\kappa+2+2\sigma+2\hat{\sigma})}+1\right]\,dx\right)^{1/m}

\displaystyle\qquad\ \ +c_{\textnormal{ab}}(\kappa+1)^{2}\lVert f\rVert_{L^{n}(\mathcal{B})}^{2}\left(\int_{\mathcal{B}}\eta^{2^{*}}\left[[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{\frac{2^{*}}{2}(\kappa+2)}+1\right]\,dx\right)^{2/2^{*}},

with
c,c_{\textnormal{ab}}\equiv c,c_{\textnormal{ab}}(n,N,\nu,\gamma,T,c_{\textnormal{b}})
. We now determine the radius
R_{*}
such that
\texttt{r}(\mathcal{B})\leq R_{*}
as in the statement of Proposition
1
. Specifically, we fix
\bar{\kappa}\geq 0
and, using the absolute continuity of the integral, determine
R_{*}\equiv R_{*}(n,N,\nu,\gamma,\linebreak T,c_{\textnormal{b}},\mathfrak{f}(\cdot),\bar{\kappa})\in(0,1)
, such that

\displaystyle c_{\textnormal{ab}}(\bar{\kappa}+1)^{2}\lVert f\rVert_{L^{n}(\mathcal{B})}^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{a0})}}{{\leq}}c_{\textnormal{ab}}(\bar{\kappa}+1)^{2}\lVert\mathfrak{f}\rVert_{L^{n}(\mathcal{B})}^{2}\leq 1/2\,.

(6.28)

With (
6.28
) being now in force, for all
\kappa\leq\bar{\kappa}
estimate (
6.27
) becomes

\displaystyle\left(\int_{\mathcal{B}}\eta^{2^{*}}\left[[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{\frac{2^{*}}{2}(\kappa+2)}+1\right]\,dx\right)^{2/2^{*}}

\displaystyle\quad\ \leq c(\kappa+1)^{2}\left(\lVert h\rVert_{L^{d}(\mathcal{B})}^{2}+1\right)

(6.29)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\left(\int_{\mathcal{B}}(\eta^{2m}+\lvert D\eta\rvert^{2m})\left[[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{m(\kappa+2+2\sigma+2\hat{\sigma})}+1\right]\,dx\right)^{1/m}

for
c\equiv c(n,N,\nu,\gamma,T,c_{\textnormal{b}})
.

6.2
Moser’s iteration in finite steps

With
1\leq i\in\mathbb{N}
, we inductively define the exponents

\kappa_{1}:=0,\quad\kappa_{i+1}:=\chi(\kappa_{i}+2)-2(1+\sigma+\hat{\sigma}),\quad s_{i}:=m(\kappa_{i}+2+2\sigma+2\hat{\sigma})\,.

(6.30)

Note that
s_{*}/n-s_{*}/d<\chi-1
if and only if
s_{*}<2n/(n-2)
, and therefore the second condition in (
6.15
) implies, again together with the first one, that

\sigma+\hat{\sigma}<\frac{s_{*}}{n}-\frac{s_{*}}{d}<\chi-1\,.

(6.31)

This, in turn, implies that
\{\kappa_{i}\}
and
\{s_{i}\}
are increasing sequences; moreover, it holds that

\begin{cases}\displaystyle\kappa_{i+1}=2\chi^{i}-2(\sigma+\hat{\sigma})\sum_{j=0}^{i-1}\chi^{j}-2\quad\mbox{ for $i\geq 1$}\\
\displaystyle s_{i+1}=2m\chi^{i}-2m(\sigma+\hat{\sigma})\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}\chi^{j}\quad\mbox{for $i\geq 2$ \ \ and \ \ $s_{2}=2m\chi=2^{*}$}.\end{cases}

Note that this implies

\begin{cases}\displaystyle s_{i+1}=\frac{2^{*}}{2}(\kappa_{i}+2)=\frac{n(\kappa_{i}+2)}{n-2}\Longrightarrow k_{i+1}\leq s_{i+1}\leq 2m\chi^{i}\\
\displaystyle k_{i+1}\leq 2\chi^{i}\,.\end{cases}

(6.32)

Again (
6.31
) implies, for
i\geq 1

\displaystyle s_{i+1}=2m\chi^{i}\left[1-\frac{(\sigma+\hat{\sigma})(1-\chi^{1-i})}{\chi-1}\right]>2m\chi^{i}\left(1-\frac{\sigma+\hat{\sigma}}{\chi-1}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\Longrightarrow\lim_{i\to\infty}s_{i+1}=\infty\,,

(6.33)

so that from the first relation in (
6.32
) it also follows that
\kappa_{i}\to\infty
.
For
0<\varsigma\leq\tau_{1}<\tau_{2}\leq\varrho
, we consider a sequence
\{B_{\varrho_{i}}\}
of shrinking, concentric balls,

B_{\tau_{1}}\Subset B_{\varrho_{i}+1}\Subset B_{\varrho_{i}}\Subset B_{\tau_{2}}
, where
\varrho_{i}:=\tau_{1}+(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})2^{-i+1}
. Note that
\{\varrho_{i}\}
is a decreasing sequence with
\varrho_{1}=\tau_{2}
and
\varrho_{i}\to\tau_{1}
, therefore it is
\bigcap_{i\in\mathbb{N}}B_{\varrho_{i}}=\overline{B_{\tau_{1}}}
and
B_{\varrho_{1}}=B_{\tau_{2}}
. Accordingly, we fix corresponding cut-off functions
\{\eta_{i}\}\subset C^{1}_{{\rm c}}(B)
with
\mathds{1}_{B_{\varrho_{i+1}}}\leq\eta_{i}\leq\mathds{1}_{B_{\varrho_{i}}}
and
\lvert D\eta_{i}\rvert\lesssim 1/(\varrho_{i}-\varrho_{i+1})\approx 2^{i}/(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})
.
Choosing
\eta\equiv\eta_{i}
in (
6.29
), elementary manipulations also based on (
6.30
) and (
6.32
) give that

\displaystyle\left(\int_{B_{\varrho_{i+1}}}\left[[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{s_{i+1}}+1\right]\,dx\right)^{1/\chi}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{\left(c\lVert h\rVert_{L^{d}(\mathcal{B})}+c\right)^{2m}2^{2mi}(\kappa_{i}+1)^{2m}}{(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})^{2m}}\int_{B_{\varrho_{i}}}\left[[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{s_{i}}+1\right]\,dx

(6.34)

holds whenever
\kappa_{i}\leq\bar{\kappa}
and
i\geq 1
, with
c\equiv c(n,N,\nu,\gamma,T,c_{\textnormal{b}})
. Finally, we set
c_{h}:=(c\lVert h\rVert_{L^{d}(\mathcal{B})}+c)^{2^{*}}
and

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{i}:=\left(\int_{B_{\varrho_{i}}}\left[[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{s_{i}}+1\right]\,dx\right)^{1/s_{i}}\,,

so that (
6.2
) (recall that
2^{*}=2m\chi
by (
6.14
)) becomes

\mathcal{G}_{i+1}\leq\left[\frac{c_{h}2^{2^{*}i}(\kappa_{i}+1)^{2^{*}}}{(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})^{2^{*}}}\right]^{\frac{1}{s_{i+1}}}\mathcal{G}_{i}^{\frac{\chi s_{i}}{s_{i+1}}}\,.

Iterating the above inequality yields that

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{i+1}\leq\prod_{j=0}^{i-1}\left[\frac{c_{h}2^{2^{*}(i-j)}(\kappa_{i-j}+1)^{2^{*}}}{(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})^{2^{*}}}\right]^{\frac{\chi^{j}}{s_{i+1}}}\mathcal{G}_{1}^{\frac{\chi^{i}s_{1}}{s_{i+1}}}\quad\mbox{holds provided $\kappa_{i}\leq\bar{\kappa}$}\,.

(6.35)

Now, from (
6.2
) we deduce that

\frac{\chi^{i+1}}{s_{i+1}}\leq\frac{\chi(\chi-1)}{2m(\chi-1-\sigma-\hat{\sigma})}\,.

(6.36)

The function
t\mapsto t/\chi^{t}
is decreasing on
[1/\log\chi,\infty)
and using this fact one sees that

\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\frac{j}{\chi^{j}}\lesssim\frac{1}{(\log\chi)^{2}}\leq\frac{c}{(\chi-1)^{2}}\,.

(6.37)

We then write

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\prod_{j=0}^{i-1}\left[\frac{c_{h}2^{2^{*}(i-j)}(\kappa_{i-j}+1)^{2^{*}}}{(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})^{2^{*}}}\right]^{\frac{\chi^{j}}{s_{i+1}}}

\displaystyle=\exp\left\{\log\left(\frac{c_{h}}{(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})^{2^{*}}}\right)\frac{1}{s_{i+1}}\sum_{j=0}^{i-1}\chi^{j}+\frac{2^{*}\log 2}{s_{i+1}}\sum_{j=0}^{i-1}(i-j)\chi^{j}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 56.9055pt+\frac{2^{*}}{s_{i+1}}\sum_{j=0}^{i-1}\chi^{j}\log(\kappa_{i-j}+1)\right\},

and note that, for every integer
i\geq 1
, we have

\frac{1}{s_{i+1}}\sum_{j=0}^{i-1}\chi^{j}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{recalls2})}}{{\leq}}c\,,\quad\frac{1}{s_{i+1}}\sum_{j=0}^{i-1}(i-j)\chi^{j}\leq c\sum_{j=0}^{i-1}\frac{i-j}{\chi^{i-j}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{recalls3})}}{{\leq}}c

and

\frac{2^{*}\log 2}{s_{i+1}}\sum_{j=0}^{i-1}\chi^{j}\log(\kappa_{i-j}+1)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{recallss}),(\ref{recalls2})}}{{\leq}}c\sum_{j=0}^{i-1}\frac{i-j}{\chi^{i-j}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{recalls3})}}{{\leq}}c\,,

where
c\equiv c(n,d,\sigma,\tilde{\sigma})
in all cases. Using the content of the last three displays yields

\prod_{j=0}^{i-1}\left[\frac{c_{h}2^{2^{*}(i-j)}(\kappa_{i-j}+1)^{2^{*}}}{(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})^{2^{*}}}\right]^{\frac{\chi^{j}}{s_{i+1}}}\leq\frac{c}{(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})^{\beta}}<\infty\,,

(6.38)

where
c\equiv c(\texttt{data},\lVert h\rVert_{L^{d}(\mathcal{B})})\geq 1
and
\beta\equiv\beta(n,d,\sigma,\hat{\sigma})\geq 1
. Note that such constants blow-up when
\chi\to 1+\sigma+\hat{\sigma}
; in particular, this happens when
d\to n
. Using (
6.38
) in (
6.35
), and keeping (
6.36
) in mind, yields

\displaystyle\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{s_{i+1}}(B_{\tau_{1}})}

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{G}_{i+1}\leq\frac{c}{(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})^{\beta}}\mathcal{G}_{1}^{\frac{\chi^{i}s_{1}}{s_{i+1}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c}{(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})^{\beta}}\left[\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{s_{1}}(B_{\tau_{2}})}^{\frac{\chi^{i}s_{1}}{s_{i+1}}}+1\right]\,,

(6.39)

with
c\equiv c(\texttt{data},\lVert h\rVert_{L^{d}(\mathcal{B})})
and
\beta\equiv\beta(n,d,\sigma,\hat{\sigma})
, for every
i\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\kappa_{i}\leq\bar{\kappa}
.

6.3
Sobolev regularity

By (
6.15
) we have
1\leq s_{*}<s_{1}=2m(1+\sigma+\hat{\sigma})
so that, for every integer index
i\geq 1
, we consider the interpolation inequality

\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{s_{1}}(B_{\tau_{2}})}\leq\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{s_{i+1}}(B_{\tau_{2}})}^{\lambda_{i+1}}\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{s_{*}}(B_{\tau_{2}})}^{1-\lambda_{i+1}}\,,

(6.40)

with
\lambda_{i+1}
being defined by

\frac{1}{s_{1}}=\frac{1-\lambda_{i+1}}{s_{*}}+\frac{\lambda_{i+1}}{s_{i+1}}\Rightarrow\lambda_{i+1}=\frac{s_{i+1}(s_{1}-s_{*})}{s_{1}(s_{i+1}-s_{*})}\,.

(6.41)

Let us show that, thanks to (
6.15
),
there exist
\bar{\vartheta}\equiv\bar{\vartheta}(n,d,\sigma,\hat{\sigma})<1
and
i_{1}\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\displaystyle i>i_{1}\Longrightarrow\frac{\lambda_{i+1}\chi^{i}s_{1}}{s_{i+1}}\leq\bar{\vartheta}<1\,.

(6.42)

Indeed, for this it is sufficient to observe that

\displaystyle\lim_{i\to\infty}\,\frac{\lambda_{i+1}\chi^{i}s_{1}}{s_{i+1}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{il-la})}}{{=}}\lim_{i\to\infty}\,\frac{\chi^{i}(s_{1}-s_{*})}{s_{i+1}-s_{*}}

\displaystyle=l_{*}:=\frac{\chi-1}{2m}\frac{2m(1+\sigma+\hat{\sigma})-s_{*}}{\chi-1-\sigma-\hat{\sigma}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{sigmasss})}}{{<}}1\,.

Note that the last inequality is actually equivalent to (
6.15
) and that (
6.15
) is in fact implied by (
4.15
) for
s_{*}=1
. Now, we consider the number
p>1
for the statement of Proposition
1
, and determine another index
k\equiv k(p)>i_{1}
such that
s_{k+1}\geq p
; accordingly, we consider the number
\kappa_{k}
related to
s_{k}
via (
6.32
). We now choose the number
\bar{\kappa}\equiv\bar{\kappa}(p)
in (
6.28
) as
\bar{\kappa}:=\kappa_{k}
, and accordingly we determine
R_{*}\equiv R_{*}(p)
via (
6.28
). It follows that (
6.2
) holds in the case
i\equiv k
and therefore we can plug (
6.40
) in it, thereby obtaining

\displaystyle\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{s_{k+1}}(B_{\tau_{1}})}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{c}{(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})^{\beta}}\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{s_{k+1}}(B_{\tau_{2}})}^{\frac{\lambda_{k+1}\chi^{k}s_{1}}{s_{k+1}}}\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{s_{*}}(B_{\tau_{2}})}^{\frac{(1-\lambda_{k+1})\chi^{k}s_{1}}{s_{k+1}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\frac{c}{(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})^{\beta}}\,.

(6.43)

On the other hand, as
k>i_{1}
, then (
6.42
) holds with
i\equiv k
; we can therefore apply
Young inequality in (
6.3
); this yields

\displaystyle\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{s_{k+1}}(B_{\tau_{1}})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{s_{k+1}}(B_{\tau_{2}})}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{c}{(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})^{\beta_{k}}}\left[\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{s_{*}}(B_{\varrho})}^{\theta_{k}}+1\right],

where

\beta_{k}:=\frac{s_{k+1}\beta}{s_{k+1}-\lambda_{k+1}\chi^{k}s_{1}}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\theta_{k}:=\frac{(1-\lambda_{k+1})\chi^{k+1}s_{1}}{s_{k+1}-\lambda_{k+1}\chi^{k}s_{1}}\,.

(6.44)

Lemma
2
with the choice
\mathcal{Z}(t)\equiv\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{s_{k+1}}(B_{t})}
, finally yields

\displaystyle\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{s_{k+1}}(B_{\varsigma})}\leq\frac{c}{(\varrho-\varsigma)^{\beta_{k}}}\left[\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{s_{*}}(B_{\varrho})}^{\theta_{k}}+1\right]\,,

with
c\equiv c(\texttt{data},\lVert h\rVert_{L^{d}(\mathcal{B})})
. This last inequality holds provided the bound in (
6.15
) holds. All in all, recalling (
5.18
)
2
, (
6.26
) and that
p\leq s_{k+1}
, completes the proof of Proposition
1
with
\beta_{p}:=\beta_{k(p)},\theta_{p}:=\theta_{k(p)}
. We remark that in (
6.16
) the exponents
\theta_{p},\beta_{p}
can be replaced by exponents
\tilde{\beta},\tilde{\theta}\equiv\tilde{\beta},\tilde{\theta}(n,d,\sigma,\hat{\sigma})
that are independent of
p
. For this, observe that

\begin{cases}\beta_{k},\beta_{p}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{nuoviesponenti})}}{{\to}}\frac{\beta}{1-l_{*}}=:\tilde{\beta}\,,\quad\lambda_{k}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{il-la})}}{{\to}}1-\frac{s_{*}}{s_{1}}\\
\theta_{k},\theta_{p}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{nuoviesponenti})}}{{\to}}\frac{\chi l_{*}s_{*}}{(s_{1}-s_{*})(1-l_{*})}:=\tilde{\theta}\,,\end{cases}

(6.45)

when
k,p\to\infty
.
The only dependence on
p
in (
6.16
) comes through the threshold radius
R_{*}
; it is
R_{*}(p)\to 0
as
p\to\infty
unless
f\equiv 0
.

Remark 8

In the proof of Proposition
1
, assume that
g_{2,\varepsilon}/g_{2,\varepsilon}\leq c_{\rm b}
as in (
5.30
), and that
\sigma=\hat{\sigma}
(compare with the assumptions of Theorem
4.4
). It is then easy to see that
\sigma+\hat{\sigma}
can be replaced by
\hat{\sigma}
, everywhere, starting from (
6.29
). This finally reflects in the same replacement in (
6.15
); in particular, we can assume
\hat{\sigma}<s_{*}/n-s_{*}/d
.

6.4
A Lipschitz bound in the homogeneous case
f\equiv 0
.

The above reasoning, eventually culminating in Proposition
1
, immediately leads to a priori Lipschitz estimates when
f\equiv 0
. The result, when combined with the approximation of Section
7
below, extends those in
M2
;
M3
;
MP
to the case of nonautonomous functionals with superlinear growth as in (
1.1
). For this, the key observation is that it is not any longer necessary to consider balls with small radii
R_{*}
as in (
6.28
), as the last term in (
6.27
) does not appear. Therefore we can take everywhere, and in particular in (
6.16
),
R_{*}=1
, independently of the value of
p
. It follows we can let
p\to\infty
in (
6.16
), and recalling (
6.45
) we conclude with

Proposition 2

Let
u\in W^{1,\gamma}(\mathcal{B};\mathbb{R}^{N})
be a solution to
(
6.1
)
, under assumptions
set
in
(
4.20
)
for
n\geq 2
and with
f\equiv 0
. Moreover, replace
(
4.15
)
by the weaker
\sigma+\hat{\sigma}<1/n-1/d
.
If
B_{\varsigma}\Subset B_{\varrho}
are concentric balls contained in
\mathcal{B}
, then

\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{\varsigma})}\leq\frac{c}{(\varrho-\varsigma)^{\tilde{\beta}}}\left[\|F_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,Du)\|_{L^{1}(B_{\varrho})}^{\tilde{\theta}}+1\right]\,,

holds with
c\equiv c(\texttt{data},\lVert h\rVert_{L^{d}(\mathcal{B})})\geq 1
,
\tilde{\beta},\tilde{\theta}\equiv\tilde{\beta},\tilde{\theta}(n,d,\sigma,\hat{\sigma})>0
.

Notice that here we are using Proposition
1
with the choice
s_{*}=1
. Note also that Proposition
1
refers to the case
n>2
. The remaining two dimensional case can be obtained via minor modifications to the proof of Proposition
1
, by choosing
2^{*}/2
large enough (see (
2.2
)) in order to get
\chi>1
in (
6.14
). Anyway, the two dimensional case
n=2
will be treated in Section
6.7
directly for the general case
f\not=0
. In that situation the proof cannot be readapted from the one of Proposition
1
as for Proposition
2
.

6.5
Caccioppoli inequality on level sets

This is in the following:

Lemma 8
(Caccioppoli inequality)

Let
u\in W^{1,\gamma}(\mathcal{B};\mathbb{R}^{N})
be a solution to
(
6.1
)
, under assumptions
set
in
(
4.20
)
for
n\geq 2
, and let
B_{r}(x_{0})\Subset\mathcal{B}
be a ball.
Then

\displaystyle\int_{B_{r/2}(x_{0})}\lvert D(G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)-\kappa)_{+}\rvert^{2}\ \,dx

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{c}{r^{2}}\lVert\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{r}(x_{0}))}^{\vartheta\sigma}\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}(G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)-\kappa)_{+}^{2}\ \,dx

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+c\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}[h(x)]^{2}[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{2(1+\sigma+\hat{\sigma})}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+c\lVert Du\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{r}(x_{0}))}^{2}\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}\lvert f\rvert^{2}\ \,dx

(6.46)

holds whenever
\kappa\geq 0
, with
c\equiv c(\texttt{data})
and
\vartheta\equiv\vartheta(\gamma)
is as in (
4.15
).

Proof

For
s\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
, we take
\varphi_{s}:=\eta^{2}(G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)-\kappa)_{+}D_{s}u
in (
6.17
), where
\eta\in C^{1}_{{\rm c}}(B_{r}(x_{0}))
satisfies
\mathds{1}_{B_{r/2}(x_{0})}\leq\eta\leq\mathds{1}_{B_{r}(x_{0})}
and
\lvert D\eta\rvert\lesssim 1/r
. Admissibility of
\varphi_{s}
follows by (
6.5
) and (
6.10
). Note that all the integrals stemming from (
6.17
) extend over
\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}:=\mathcal{B}\cap\{G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)>\kappa\}
; in particular, by the very definition of
G_{\varepsilon}
, we can always restrict to the case it is
|Du|>T
and
g_{1,\varepsilon}(\cdot,|Du|)>0
(recall (
4.6
)). We start expanding the terms resulting from (
6.17
)

\displaystyle\sum_{s=1}^{n}

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}}\partial_{z}a_{\varepsilon}(x,Du)DD_{s}u\cdot D\varphi_{s}\,dx

\displaystyle=\sum_{s=1}^{n}\int_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}}\eta^{2}(G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)-\kappa)_{+}\partial_{z}a_{\varepsilon}(x,Du)DD_{s}u\cdot DD_{s}u\,dx

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{s=1}^{n}\int_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}}\eta^{2}\partial_{z}a_{\varepsilon}(x,Du)DD_{s}u\cdot\left[D_{s}u\otimes D(G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)-\kappa)_{+}\right]\,dx

\displaystyle\quad+2\sum_{s=1}^{n}\int_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}}\eta(G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)-\kappa)_{+}\partial_{z}a_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)DD_{s}u\cdot D_{s}u\otimes D\eta\,dx

\displaystyle=:\mbox{(IV)}_{z}+\mbox{(V)}_{z}+\mbox{(VI)}_{z}\,.

(6.47)

Moreover, it is

\displaystyle\sum_{s=1}^{n}\int_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}}\partial_{x_{s}}a_{\varepsilon}(x,Du)\cdot D\varphi_{s}\,dx

\displaystyle=\sum_{s=1}^{n}\int_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}}\eta^{2}(G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)-\kappa)_{+}\partial_{x_{s}}a_{\varepsilon}(x,Du)\cdot DD_{s}u\,dx

\displaystyle+\sum_{s=1}^{n}\int_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}}\eta^{2}\partial_{x_{s}}a_{\varepsilon}(x,Du)\cdot\left(D_{s}u\otimes D(G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)-\kappa)_{+}\right)\,dx

\displaystyle+2\sum_{s=1}^{n}\int_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}}\eta(G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)-\kappa)_{+}\partial_{x_{s}}a_{\varepsilon}(x,Du)\cdot\left(D_{s}u\otimes D\eta\right)\,dx

\displaystyle=:\mbox{(IV)}_{x}+\mbox{(V)}_{x}+\mbox{(VI)}_{x}\,.

(6.48)

By (
6.9
) we have

\mbox{(IV)}_{z}\geq\int_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}}\eta^{2}(G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)-\kappa)_{+}g_{1,\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)\lvert D^{2}u\rvert^{2}\,dx=:\mathcal{S}_{3}

(6.49)

and for later use we also define

\mathcal{S}_{4}:=\int_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}}\eta^{2}\lvert D(G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)-k)_{+}\rvert^{2}\,dx\,.

(6.50)

We then consider two different cases.

Case 1:
1<\gamma<2
in
(
4.4
)
, and therefore it is
\vartheta=2
.
We proceed estimating the terms
\mbox{(V)}_{z}
and
\mbox{(VI)}_{z}
. The estimate for the term (V)
z
is similar to the one for (II)
z
in (
6.20
), see in particular those for the terms
\mbox{(II)}_{z,3}
and
\mbox{(II)}_{z,4}
; indeed, again using (
5.15
), (
5.29
), (
5.31
), (
6.3
) and (
6.13
), we have

\displaystyle\mbox{(V)}_{z}

\displaystyle\geq\mathcal{S}_{4}-c\int_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}}\eta^{2}h(x)[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(x,s)]^{1+\sigma+\hat{\sigma}}\lvert D(G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)-k)_{+}\rvert\,dx

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{S}_{4}-c\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}[h(x)]^{2}[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{2(1+\sigma+\hat{\sigma})}\,dx\,,

with
c\equiv c(n,N,\nu,\gamma,c_{\textnormal{b}})
. Using (
6.7
)-(
6.12
), we then have

\displaystyle\mbox{(VI)}_{z}

\displaystyle=2\int_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}}\eta(G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)-\kappa)_{+}\frac{\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)}{g_{1,\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)}DG_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)\cdot D\eta\,dx

\displaystyle\qquad+2\sum_{\alpha=1}^{N}\int_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}}\eta\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{D}}(|Du|)(G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)-\kappa)_{+}\frac{\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)\lvert Du\rvert}{g_{1,\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)\lvert Du\rvert^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 99.58464pt\times(DG_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)\cdot Du^{\alpha})(Du^{\alpha}\cdot D\eta)\,dx

\displaystyle\qquad-2\int_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}}\eta(G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)-\kappa)_{+}\frac{\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)}{g_{1,\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)}

\displaystyle\hskip 99.58464pt\times\left(\int_{T}^{\lvert Du\rvert}\partial_{x}g_{1,\varepsilon}(x,t)t\,dt\cdot D\eta\right)\,dx

\displaystyle\qquad-2\sum_{\alpha=1}^{N}\int_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}}\eta\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{D}}(|Du|)(G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)-\kappa)_{+}\frac{\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)\lvert Du\rvert}{g_{1,\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)\lvert Du\rvert^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 71.13188pt\times\left(\int_{T}^{\lvert Du\rvert}\partial_{x}g_{1,\varepsilon}(x,t)t\,dt\cdot Du^{\alpha}\right)(Du^{\alpha}\cdot D\eta)\,dx\,.

Using (
5.29
), (
5.31
), (
6.3
)-(
6.4
), and Young inequality, we have

\displaystyle|\mbox{(VI)}_{z}|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4}\mathcal{S}_{4}+\frac{c}{r^{2}}\lVert\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{r}(x_{0}))}^{2\sigma}\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}(G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)-\kappa)_{+}^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad+c\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}[h(x)]^{2}[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{2(1+\hat{\sigma})}\,dx\,,

(6.51)

where
c\equiv c(n,N,\nu,\gamma,c_{\textnormal{b}})
.
Gathering the content of displays from (
6.47
) to (
6.51
), and using them in (
6.17
), some further elementary estimations we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{3}+\mathcal{S}_{4}

\displaystyle\leq c|\mbox{(IV)}_{x}|+c|\mbox{(V)}_{x}|+c|\mbox{(VI)}_{x}|

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{c}{r^{2}}\lVert\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{r}(x_{0}))}^{\vartheta\sigma}\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}(G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)-\kappa)_{+}^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad+c\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}[h(x)]^{2}[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{2(1+\sigma+\hat{\sigma})}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad+c\sum_{s=1}^{n}\int_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}}\lvert f\cdot D_{s}\varphi_{s}\rvert\ \,dx

(6.52)

where
\vartheta=2
and
c\equiv c(n,N,\nu,\gamma,c_{\textnormal{b}})
.

Case 2:
\gamma\geq 2
in
(
4.4
)
, and therefore it is
\vartheta=1
. In this case we use that the function
t\mapsto\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,t)
is non-decreasing, so that
\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(\cdot)
is non-negative (when it exists). We note that

\displaystyle\mbox{(V)}_{z}+\mbox{(VI)}_{z}

\displaystyle\quad\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{a8})}}{{=}}\sum_{s=1}^{n}\int_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}}\eta^{2}g_{1,\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)\lvert Du\rvert\partial_{z}a_{\varepsilon}(x,Du)DD_{s}u\cdot D_{s}u\otimes D\lvert Du\rvert\,dx

\displaystyle\qquad+2\sum_{s=1}^{n}\int_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}}\eta(G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)-k)_{+}\partial_{z}a_{\varepsilon}(x,Du)DD_{s}u\cdot D_{s}u\otimes D\eta\,dx

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{s=1}^{n}\int_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}}\eta^{2}\partial_{z}a_{\varepsilon}(x,Du)DD_{s}u\cdot D_{s}u\otimes\int_{T}^{\lvert Du\rvert}\partial_{x}g_{1,\varepsilon}(x,t)t\,dt\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\ \ =:\mbox{(V)}_{z,1}+\mbox{(VI)}_{z}+\mbox{(V)}_{z,2}\,.

(6.53)

In turn, we have

\displaystyle\mbox{(V)}_{z,1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{diso2})}}{{=}}\int_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}}\eta^{2}g_{1,\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}(x,|Du|)|D|Du||^{2}\lvert Du\rvert^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\ +\sum_{\alpha=1}^{N}\int_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}}\eta^{2}g_{1,\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{D}}(|Du|)\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(x,|Du|)|Du|(D|Du|\cdot Du^{\alpha})^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{a1}),\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(\cdot)\geq 0}}{{\geq}}\int_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}}\eta^{2}[g_{1,\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)\lvert Du\rvert]^{2}|D|Du||^{2}\,dx\,.

(6.54)

For
\mbox{(VI)}_{z}
, keeping in mind the identity in the last display, we again use (
6.7
) and
\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(\cdot)\geq 0
to estimate via Young inequality as follows:

\displaystyle 4|\mbox{(VI)}_{z}|\leq 8\int_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}}\eta(G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)-k)_{+}\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)|Du||D|Du|\cdot D\eta|\,dx

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\ +8\sum_{\alpha=1}^{N}\int_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}}\eta(G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)-k)_{+}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{D}}(|Du|)\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(x,|Du|)

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\times|(D|Du|\cdot Du^{\alpha})(Du^{\alpha}\cdot D\eta)|\,dx

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\mbox{(V)}_{z,1}+c\int_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}}\frac{(G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)-k)_{+}^{2}}{g_{1,\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)}\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}(x,|Du|)|D\eta|^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+c\sum_{\alpha=1}^{N}\int_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}}\frac{(G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)-k)_{+}^{2}}{g_{1,\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{D}}(|Du|)\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(x,|Du|)\frac{(Du^{\alpha}\cdot D\eta)^{2}}{|Du|}\,dx

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\mbox{(V)}_{z,1}+\frac{c}{r^{2}}\lVert\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{r}(x_{0}))}^{\sigma}\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}(G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)-\kappa)_{+}^{2}\,dx\,.

As for
\mbox{(V)}_{z,2}
, by letting

\mathcal{I}:=\int_{T}^{\lvert Du\rvert}\partial_{x}g_{1,\varepsilon}(x,t)t\,dt\Longrightarrow|\mathcal{I}|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{z10})}}{{\leq}}ch(x)[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{1+\hat{\sigma}}

(6.55)

we have, again using that
\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(\cdot)\geq 0

\displaystyle 4|\mbox{(V)}_{z,2}|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{diso2})}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle 4\int_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}}\eta^{2}\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)|Du|\left|D|Du|\cdot\mathcal{I}\right|\,dx

(6.56)

\displaystyle\quad+4\sum_{\alpha=1}^{N}\int_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}}\eta^{2}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{D}}(|Du|)\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(x,|Du|)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\times|(D|Du|\cdot Du^{\alpha})(Du^{\alpha}\cdot\mathcal{I})|\,dx

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{VVVV})}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\mbox{(V)}_{z,1}+c\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}\frac{\eta^{2}[\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}(x,|Du|)+\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(x,|Du|)|Du|]}{g_{1,\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)}|\mathcal{I}|^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{a2}_{2}}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\mbox{(V)}_{z,1}+c\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}\frac{g_{2,\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)}{g_{1,\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)}\left|\mathcal{I}\right|^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{z9}),(\ref{letting})}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\mbox{(V)}_{z,1}+c\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}[h(x)]^{2}[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{\sigma+2(1+\hat{\sigma})}\,dx.

On the other hand, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{4}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{a8})}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle c\int_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}}\eta^{2}\left([g_{1,\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)\lvert Du\rvert]^{2}|D|Du||^{2}+|\mathcal{I}|^{2}\right)\,dx

(6.57)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{letting})}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle c\int_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}}\eta^{2}\left([g_{1,\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)\lvert Du\rvert]^{2}|D|Du||^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\left.+|h(x)|^{2}[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{2(1+\hat{\sigma})}\right)\,dx

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{VVVV})}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle c\mbox{(V)}_{z,1}+c\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}[h(x)]^{2}[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{2(1+\hat{\sigma})}\,dx\,.

Assembling the content of displays from (
6.53
) to (
6.57
) and using it in (
6.17
), we again conclude with (
6.52
), but this time with
\vartheta=1
. We proceed estimating the
x
-terms coming from (
6.52
) (these have been defined in (
6.48
)), using
\eqref{growthd}_{3,4}
, (
5.37
) as follows:

\displaystyle\lvert\mbox{(IV)}_{x}\rvert+\lvert\mbox{(V)}_{x}\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\bar{\varepsilon}\mathcal{S}_{3}+\bar{\varepsilon}\mathcal{S}_{4}+\frac{c}{\bar{\varepsilon}}\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}\eta^{2}[h(x)]^{2}[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{2(1+\sigma)}\,dx\,,

\displaystyle\lvert\mbox{(VI)}_{x}\rvert

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{z11}_{1}}}{{\leq}}\frac{c}{r^{2}}\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}(G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)-\kappa)_{+}^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\qquad\ \ +c\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}[h(x)]^{2}[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{2(1+\sigma)}\,dx\,,

with
c\equiv c(\texttt{data})
and arbitrary
\bar{\varepsilon}\in(0,1)
. Finally, the estimate of the terms involving
f
can be done by using Young inequality and (
5.27
) as follows:

\displaystyle\sum_{s=1}^{n}\int_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}}\lvert f\cdot D_{s}\varphi_{s}\rvert\,dx\leq\bar{\varepsilon}\mathcal{S}_{3}+\bar{\varepsilon}\mathcal{S}_{4}+\frac{c}{\bar{\varepsilon}}\int_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}}|D\eta|^{2}(G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)-\kappa)_{+}^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{c}{\bar{\varepsilon}}\int_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}}\eta^{2}\left[\frac{(G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)-\kappa)_{+}}{g_{1,\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)}+\lvert Du\rvert^{2}\right]\lvert f\rvert^{2}\,dx\,

\displaystyle\quad\leq\bar{\varepsilon}\mathcal{S}_{3}+\bar{\varepsilon}\mathcal{S}_{4}+\frac{c}{\bar{\varepsilon}r^{2}}\int_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}}(G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)-\kappa)_{+}^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{c}{\bar{\varepsilon}}\lVert Du\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{r_{0}}(x_{0}))}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}}\eta^{2}\lvert f\rvert^{2}\,dx\,,

(6.58)

for
c\equiv c(\texttt{data})
and arbitrary
\bar{\varepsilon}\in(0,1)
. Collecting the estimates in the last three displays to (
6.52
), recalling that
recalling that
\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(\cdot)\geq 1
, and selecting
\bar{\varepsilon}>0
sufficiently small in order to reabsorb terms, we complete the proof of Lemma
8
.

6.6
Nonlinear iterations

In this section we finally derive pointwise gradient bounds.
This goes via Lemma
9
and Proposition
3
below.

Lemma 9

Let
u\in W^{1,\gamma}(\mathcal{B};\mathbb{R}^{N})
be a solution to
(
6.1
)
, under assumptions
set
in
(
4.20
)
for
n>2
. If
B_{2r_{0}}(x_{0})\Subset\mathcal{B}
is a ball such that
x_{0}
is a Lebesgue point of
|Du|
, then

\displaystyle G_{\varepsilon}(x_{0},\lvert Du(x_{0})\rvert)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\kappa+c\lVert\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{r}(x_{0}))}^{n\vartheta\sigma/4}\left(\mathop{\int\hskip-10.50005pt-\,\!\!\!}\nolimits_{B_{r_{0}}(x_{0})}(G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)-\kappa)_{+}^{2}\,dx\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\quad\ \ \ \ +c\lVert\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{r}(x_{0}))}^{(n-2)\vartheta\sigma/4}

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\ \times\left[\mathbf{P}_{1}^{\mathfrak{h}}(x_{0},2r_{0})+\lVert Du\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{r_{0}}(x_{0}))}\mathbf{P}_{1}^{f}(x_{0},2r_{0})\right]\,,

(6.59)

holds for all
\kappa\geq 0
, with
c\equiv c(\texttt{data})
, where
\vartheta\equiv\vartheta(\gamma)
is as in (
4.15
),
and

\displaystyle\mathfrak{h}(x):=h(x)[\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{1+\sigma+\hat{\sigma}}\,.

(6.60)

Proof

Note that we can assume that
|Du(x_{0})|>T
, otherwise (
9
) is trivial by the very definition of
G_{\varepsilon}
; this obviously implies
\|Du\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{r}(x_{0}))}>T
. Let us first note that
x_{0}
is also a Lebesgue point of
x\mapsto G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du(x)\rvert)
and it is

\lim_{r\to 0}\mathop{\int\hskip-10.50005pt-\,\!\!\!}\nolimits_{B_{r}(x_{0})}G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du(x)\rvert)\,dx=G_{\varepsilon}(x_{0},\lvert Du(x_{0})\rvert)\,,

(6.61)

i.e., the right-hand side denotes the precise representative of
G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du(\cdot)\rvert)
at the point
x_{0}
. Indeed, note that

\displaystyle\limsup_{r\to 0}\mathop{\int\hskip-10.50005pt-\,\!\!\!}\nolimits_{B_{r}(x_{0})}|G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du(x)\rvert)-G_{\varepsilon}(x_{0},\lvert Du(x_{0})\rvert)|\,dx

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\limsup_{r\to 0}\mathop{\int\hskip-10.50005pt-\,\!\!\!}\nolimits_{B_{r}(x_{0})}|G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du(x)\rvert)-G_{\varepsilon}(x_{0},\lvert Du(x)\rvert)|\,dx

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\limsup_{r\to 0}\mathop{\int\hskip-10.50005pt-\,\!\!\!}\nolimits_{B_{r}(x_{0})}|G_{\varepsilon}(x_{0},\lvert Du(x)\rvert)-G_{\varepsilon}(x_{0},\lvert Du(x_{0})\rvert)|\,dx

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad=:\limsup_{r\to 0}\mathcal{C}_{1}(r)+\limsup_{r\to 0}\mathcal{C}_{2}(r)\,.

(6.62)

As
x_{0}
is a Lebesgue point for
Du
,
t\mapsto G_{\varepsilon}(x_{0},t)
is locally Lipschitz-regular, and
Du
is locally bounded, we have

\lim_{r\to 0}\,\mathcal{C}_{2}(r)\lesssim\lim_{r\to 0}\mathop{\int\hskip-10.50005pt-\,\!\!\!}\nolimits_{B_{r}(x_{0})}|\lvert Du(x)\rvert-\lvert Du(x_{0})\rvert|\,dx=0\,.

(6.63)

As for the term
\mathcal{C}_{1}(\cdot)
, we have, also using Fubini’s

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{1}(r)\leq\mathop{\int\hskip-10.50005pt-\,\!\!\!}\nolimits_{B_{r}(x_{0})}\int_{T}^{\max\{|Du(x)|,T\}}|g_{1,\varepsilon}(x,s)-g_{1,\varepsilon}(x_{0},s)|s\,ds\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\leq\int_{T}^{\|Du\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{r}(x_{0}))}}\mathop{\int\hskip-10.50005pt-\,\!\!\!}\nolimits_{B_{r}(x_{0})}|g_{1,\varepsilon}(x,s)-g_{1,\varepsilon}(x_{0},s)|\,dx\,s\,ds

\displaystyle\quad\leq\|Du\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{r}(x_{0}))}^{2}\sup_{s\in[T,\|Du\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{r}(x_{0}))}]}\mathop{\int\hskip-10.50005pt-\,\!\!\!}\nolimits_{B_{r}(x_{0})}|g_{1,\varepsilon}(x,s)-g_{1,\varepsilon}(x_{0},s)|\,dx.

Recall that
g_{1}(\cdot)
is assumed to be continuous on
\Omega\times(0,\infty)
. By the definition in (
5.7
), this implies that also
g_{1,\varepsilon}
is continuous and therefore it is uniformly continuous on
\overline{B_{r_{0}}(x_{0})}\times[T,\|Du\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{r}(x_{0}))}]
. This and the content of the last display it is sufficient to infer that
\mathcal{C}_{1}(r)\to 0
as
r\to 0
. This fact, together with (
6.62
)-(
6.63
), yields (
6.61
).
Thanks to (
8
) we can verify (
1
) with the choices
v(\cdot)\equiv G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du(\cdot)\rvert)
,

f_{1}\equiv\mathfrak{h}
,
f_{2}\equiv f
,
M_{1}\equiv\lVert\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{r}(x_{0}))}^{\vartheta\sigma/2}
,
M_{2}\equiv 1
and
M_{3}\equiv\lVert Du\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{r_{0}}(x_{0}))}
. Applying Lemma
1
, inequality (
1
) yields (
9
).

Proposition 3

Let
u\in W^{1,\gamma}(\mathcal{B};\mathbb{R}^{N})
be a solution to
(
6.1
)
, under assumptions
set
in
(
4.20
)
for
n>2
. There exists a positive radius
R_{*}\equiv R_{*}(\texttt{data},\mathfrak{f}(\cdot))\leq 1
such that if
\texttt{r}(\mathcal{B})\leq R_{*}
and
B_{\varsigma}\Subset B_{\varrho}
are concentric balls contained in
\mathcal{B}
, then

\displaystyle[E_{\varepsilon}(\lVert Du\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{\varsigma})})]^{\gamma}+\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{\varsigma})}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{c}{(\varrho-\varsigma)^{\beta}}\left[\|F_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,Du)\|_{L^{1}(B_{\varrho})}^{\theta}+\lVert f\rVert_{L(n,1)(B_{\varrho})}^{\theta}+1\right]

(6.64)

holds with

c\equiv c(\texttt{data},\lVert h\rVert_{L^{d}(\mathcal{B})})\geq 1
and
\beta,\theta\equiv\beta,\theta(n,d,\gamma,\sigma,\hat{\sigma})>0
.

Proof

We take numbers
