\tau,p\equiv\tau,p(n,d,\sigma,\hat{\sigma})
such that

\displaystyle 0<\tau<d-n\quad\mbox{and}\quad p:=\frac{(1+\sigma+\hat{\sigma})(n+\tau)d}{d-n-\tau}\,,

(6.65)

where
d>n
is the exponent from (
4.15
), and fix
R_{*}\equiv R_{*}(\texttt{data},\mathfrak{f}(\cdot))>0
as the radius from Proposition
1
, so that (
6.16
) holds such
p
. With
B_{\varsigma}\Subset B_{\varrho}
being the balls considered in the statement, with no loss of generality we can assume that
\lVert Du\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{\varsigma})}\geq T

otherwise the assertion in (
3
) is trivial by choosing
c
large enough. Let
\varrho_{1}:=\varsigma+{(\varrho-\varsigma)/2}
and consider concentric balls
B_{\varsigma}\Subset B_{\tau_{1}}\Subset B_{\tau_{2}}\Subset B_{\varrho_{1}}\Subset B_{\varrho}
, a point
x_{0}\in B_{\tau_{1}}
which is a Lebesgue point for
|Du|
, and
r_{0}:=(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})/8
, so that
B_{2r_{0}}(x_{0})\Subset B_{\tau_{2}}
. Needless to say, a.e. point in
B_{\tau_{1}}
qualifies. By (
6.26
)
we find

\displaystyle\lVert\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{r_{0}}(x_{0}))}\leq(T^{2}+1)^{\gamma/2}\left[\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{\tau_{2}})}+1\right].

(6.66)

This and (
5.19
)
1
imply

\displaystyle\lVert Du\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{\tau_{2}})}\leq c\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{\tau_{2}})}^{1/\gamma}+c\,,

(6.67)

for
c\equiv c(\nu,\gamma,T)
. We then apply (
9
)
on
B_{r_{0}}(x_{0})
,
and also using (
6.66
)-(
6.67
) and Hölder inequality (by (
6.65
) it is
p\geq 2
), we obtain

\displaystyle G_{\varepsilon}(x_{0},\lvert Du(x_{0})\rvert)

\displaystyle\leq cr_{0}^{-n/p}\left[\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{\tau_{2}})}^{n\vartheta\sigma/4}+1\right]\|G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\|_{L^{p}(B_{r_{0}}(x_{0}))}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+c\left[\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{\tau_{2}})}^{(n-2)\vartheta\sigma/4}+1\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad\times\left[\mathbf{P}_{1}^{\mathfrak{h}}(x_{0},2r_{0})+\lVert Du\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{\tau_{2}})}\mathbf{P}_{1}^{f}(x_{0},2r_{0})\right],

(6.68)

with
c\equiv c(\texttt{data})
. With (
6.16
) (where we take
\xi\equiv r_{0}
and
\varrho\equiv 2r_{0}
) we further bound

\|G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\|_{L^{p}(B_{r_{0}}(x_{0}))}\leq cr_{0}^{-\beta_{p}}\left[\|F_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,Du)\|_{L^{1}(B_{\varrho})}^{\theta_{p}}+1\right],

for
c\equiv c(\texttt{data},\lVert h\rVert_{L^{d}(\mathcal{B})})
. Using also (
6.67
) in (
Proof
), recalling that
x_{0}\in B_{\tau_{1}}
is a arbitrary Lebesgue point for
|Du|
, we obtain

\displaystyle\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{\tau_{1}})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c}{(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})^{n/p+\beta_{p}}}\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{\tau_{2}})}^{n\vartheta\sigma/4}\left[\lVert F_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,Du)\rVert_{L^{1}(B_{\varrho})}^{\theta_{p}}+1\right]

\displaystyle\quad+c\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{\tau_{2}})}^{(n-2)\vartheta\sigma/4}\|\mathbf{P}_{1}^{\mathfrak{h}}(\cdot,(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})/4)\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{\tau_{1}})}

\displaystyle\quad+c\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{\tau_{2}})}^{(n-2)\vartheta\sigma/4+1/\gamma}\|\mathbf{P}_{1}^{f}(\cdot,(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})/4)\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{\tau_{1}})}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{c}{(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})^{n/p+\beta_{p}}}\left[\lVert F_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,Du)\rVert_{L^{1}(B_{\varrho})}^{\theta_{p}}+1\right]

\displaystyle\quad+c\|\mathbf{P}_{1}^{\mathfrak{h}}(\cdot,(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})/4)\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{\tau_{1}})}+c\|\mathbf{P}_{1}^{f}(\cdot,(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})/4)\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{\tau_{1}})}\,,

(6.69)

where
c\equiv c(\texttt{data},\lVert h\rVert_{L^{d}(\mathcal{B})})
. Now, observe that (
4.15
) implies

n\sigma\vartheta/4<1\quad\mbox{and}\quad(n-2)\sigma\vartheta/4<1-1/\gamma\,,

(6.70)

therefore we can apply Young inequality in (
Proof
)
to end up with

\displaystyle\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{\tau_{1}})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{\tau_{2}})}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{c}{(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})^{\beta_{*}}}\left[\lVert F_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,Du)\rVert_{L^{1}(B_{\varrho})}^{\theta_{*}}+1\right]

\displaystyle\quad+c\|\mathbf{P}_{1}^{\mathfrak{h}}(\cdot,(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})/4)\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{\tau_{1}})}^{\theta_{*}}

\displaystyle\quad+c\|\mathbf{P}_{1}^{f}(\cdot,(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})/4)\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{\tau_{1}})}^{\theta_{*}}+c\,,

(6.71)

where
c\equiv c(\texttt{data},\lVert h\rVert_{L^{d}(\mathcal{B})})
where it is
\beta_{*},\theta_{*}\equiv\beta_{*},\theta_{*}(n,d,\gamma,\sigma,\hat{\sigma})>0
.
Inequality (
Proof
) allows to apply Lemma
2
with the obvious choice
\mathcal{Z}(t):=\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{t})}
, and this leads to

\displaystyle\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{\varsigma})}

\displaystyle\,\leq\frac{c}{(\varrho-\varsigma)^{\beta_{*}}}\left[\lVert F_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,Du)\rVert_{L^{1}(B_{\varrho})}^{\theta_{*}}+1\right]

\displaystyle\quad+c\|\mathbf{P}_{1}^{\mathfrak{h}}(\cdot,(\varrho-\varsigma)/4)\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{\varrho_{1}})}^{\theta_{*}}

\displaystyle\quad+c\|\mathbf{P}_{1}^{f}(\cdot,(\varrho-\varsigma)/4)\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{\varrho_{1}})}^{\theta_{*}}+c

(6.72)

for
c\equiv c(\texttt{data},\lVert h\rVert_{L^{d}(\mathcal{B})})
. By using (
3.1
)
1
we infer

\|\mathbf{P}_{1}^{f}(\cdot,(\varrho-\varsigma)/4)\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{\varrho_{1}})}\leq c\lVert f\rVert_{L(n,1)(B_{\varrho})}\,.

Moreover, with
\varrho_{2}:=\varrho_{1}+(\varrho-\varsigma)/4
; also using (
6.65
) and Hölder inequality yields (recall that
s_{*}=1
)

\displaystyle\hskip-1.42262pt\|\mathbf{P}_{1}^{\mathfrak{h}}(\cdot,(\varrho-\varsigma)/4)\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{\varrho_{1}})}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{potlore}_{1}}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle c\lVert\mathfrak{h}\rVert_{L(n,1)(B_{\varrho_{2}})}

(6.73)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{normine})}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle c(\tau)\lVert\mathfrak{h}\rVert_{L^{n+\tau}(B_{\varrho_{2}})}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{fk0})}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle c\lVert h\rVert_{L^{d}(B_{\varrho})}\lVert\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{p}(B_{\varrho_{2}})}^{1+\sigma+\hat{\sigma}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{a44})}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\frac{c\lVert h\rVert_{L^{d}(B_{\varrho})}\left[\lVert F_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,Du)\rVert_{L^{1}(B_{\varrho})}^{\theta_{p}(1+\sigma+\hat{\sigma})}+1\right]}{(\varrho-\varsigma)^{\beta_{p}(1+\sigma+\hat{\sigma})}}

where
c\equiv c(\texttt{data},\lVert h\rVert_{L^{d}(\mathcal{B})})
. We have applied Proposition
1
with the choice
s_{*}=1
, so that (
6.15
) is verified by the assumption (
4.15
)
1
. Inserting the above two estimates in (
Proof
) and recalling also (
5.11
) and (
5.19
)
1
, we finally end up with (
3
), where
\beta:=\max\{\beta_{*},\beta_{p}\theta_{*}(1+\sigma+\hat{\sigma})\}
and
\theta:=\max\{\theta_{*},\theta_{p}\theta_{*}(1+\sigma+\hat{\sigma})\}.

6.7
The case
n=2

Here we consider the missing two-dimensional case. We start with the following lemma, which is a hybrid counterpart of Proposition
1
, in the sense that the a priori estimate involved still contains the
L^{\infty}
-norm of
Du
in the right-hand side. We recall that the number
m
has been defined in (
6.14
).

Lemma 10

Let
u\in W^{1,\gamma}(\mathcal{B};\mathbb{R}^{N})
be a solution to
(
6.1
)
under assumptions
set
in
(
4.20
)
for
n=2
, where we replace
(
4.15
)
2
by

\sigma+\hat{\sigma}<\frac{s_{*}}{2}-\frac{s_{*}}{d}=\frac{s_{*}}{2m},\ \ \mbox{for some $s_{*}$ such that}\ 1\leq s_{*}<2m(1+\sigma+\hat{\sigma})\,.

(6.74)

If
B_{\varsigma}\Subset B_{\varrho}
are concentric balls contained in
\mathcal{B}
, then, for every
p\geq 1
, there holds

\displaystyle\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{p}(B_{\varsigma})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c}{(\varrho-\varsigma)^{\beta_{p}}}\left[\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{s_{*}}(B_{\varrho})}^{\theta_{p}}+1\right]

\displaystyle\quad+c\lVert Du\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{\varrho})}\|f\|_{L^{2}(B_{\varrho})}

(6.75)

with
c\equiv c(\texttt{data},\lVert h\rVert_{L^{d}(\mathcal{B})},p)\geq 1
,
\beta_{p},\theta_{p}\equiv\beta_{p},\theta_{p}(d,\sigma,\hat{\sigma},p)>0
.

Proof

We can confine ourselves to prove (
6.75
) for sufficiently large
p
, and we consider

\displaystyle p>\max\left\{2m(1+\sigma+\hat{\sigma}),\frac{2ms_{*}}{s_{*}-2m(\sigma+\hat{\sigma})}\right\}=\frac{2ms_{*}}{s_{*}-2m(\sigma+\hat{\sigma})}\,.

(6.76)

The last equality comes from the second inequality in (
6.74
).
Note that such a choice is possible thanks to (
6.74
), making the denominator of the last quantity different than zero. In the following lines all the balls will be concentric to
B_{\varrho}
. We look back at the proof of Proposition
1
, take
\kappa=0
to obtain the test function
\varphi_{s}=\eta^{2}G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)D_{s}u
for
s\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
, and perform exactly the same calculations made there up to (
6.23
). For the terms
\mbox{(I)}_{f}
-
\mbox{(III)}_{f}
involving the right-hand side
f
, we note that, as
\kappa=0
, the test maps
\varphi_{s}
used in the proof of Propositions
1
and Lemma
8
do coincide (actually, we take
\kappa=0
in both Propositions
1
and Lemma
8
). Therefore we can use estimate (
6.58
),
where
c\equiv c(\texttt{data})
and
\bar{\varepsilon}\in(0,1)
; here
\mathcal{S}_{3}
and
\mathcal{S}_{4}
have been defined in (
6.49
) and (
6.50
), respectively. All together, choosing
\bar{\varepsilon}>0
small enough and re-absorbing terms in a standard way, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{3}+\mathcal{S}_{4}

\displaystyle\leq c(\lVert h\rVert_{L^{d}(\mathcal{B})}^{2}+1)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\left(\int_{\mathcal{B}}(\eta^{2m}+\lvert D\eta\rvert^{2m})\left[[G_{\varepsilon}(x,\lvert Du\rvert)]^{2m(1+\sigma+\hat{\sigma})}+1\right]\,dx\right)^{1/m}

\displaystyle\quad+c\lVert Du\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{\varrho})}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{B}}\eta^{2}\lvert f\rvert^{2}\,dx\,,

(6.77)

for
c\equiv c(\texttt{data})
. As it is
|D\eta|\lesssim 1/(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})
, elementary manipulations on (
Proof
) yield

\displaystyle\lVert D(\eta G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert))\rVert_{L^{2}(B_{\tau_{2}})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c(\lVert h\rVert_{L^{d}(\mathcal{B})}+1)^{2}}{(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})^{2}}\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{2m(1+\sigma+\hat{\sigma})}(B_{\tau_{2}})}^{2(1+\sigma+\hat{\sigma})}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{c(\lVert h\rVert_{L^{d}(\mathcal{B})}+1)^{2}}{(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})^{2}}+c\lVert Du\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{\varrho})}^{2}\int_{B_{\tau_{2}}}\lvert f\rvert^{2}\,dx

so that Sobolev embedding gives

\displaystyle\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{p}(B_{\tau_{1}})}

\displaystyle\leq c_{p}\tau_{2}^{2/p}\lVert D(\eta G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert))\rVert_{L^{2}(B_{\tau_{2}})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c}{(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})}\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{2m(1+\sigma+\hat{\sigma})}(B_{\tau_{2}})}^{1+\sigma+\hat{\sigma}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{c}{(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})}+c\lVert Du\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{\varrho})}\|f\|_{L^{2}(B_{\tau_{2}})}\,,

(6.78)

with
c\equiv c(\texttt{data},\|h\|_{L^{d}(\mathcal{B})})
. With
\lambda_{p}\in(0,1)
being defined through

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2m(1+\sigma+\hat{\sigma})}=\frac{1-\lambda_{p}}{s_{*}}+\frac{\lambda_{p}}{p}\Rightarrow\lambda_{p}=\frac{p[2m(1+\sigma+\hat{\sigma})-s_{*}]}{2m(p-s_{*})(1+\sigma+\hat{\sigma})}\,,

using the interpolation inequality

\displaystyle\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{2m(1+\sigma+\hat{\sigma})}(B_{\tau_{2}})}\leq\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{p}(B_{\tau_{2}})}^{\lambda_{p}}\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{s_{*}}(B_{\tau_{2}})}^{1-\lambda_{p}}

in (
Proof
), we get

\displaystyle\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{p}(B_{\tau_{1}})}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{c}{(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})}\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{p}(B_{\tau_{2}})}^{\lambda_{p}(1+\sigma+\hat{\sigma})}\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{s_{*}}(B_{\tau_{2}})}^{(1-\lambda_{p})(1+\sigma+\hat{\sigma})}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\frac{c}{(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})}+c\lVert Du\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{\varrho})}\|f\|_{L^{2}(B_{\tau_{2}})}\,.

Using (
6.74
) and (
6.76
), we see that
\lambda_{p}(1+\sigma+\hat{\sigma})<1
, thus Young inequality
gives

\displaystyle\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{p}(B_{\tau_{1}})}\leq\frac{1}{2}\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{p}(B_{\tau_{2}})}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{c}{(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})^{\beta_{p}}}\left[\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{s_{*}}(B_{\tau_{2}})}^{\theta_{p}}+1\right]+c\lVert Du\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{\varrho})}\|f\|_{L^{2}(B_{\tau_{2}})}

with
c\equiv c(\texttt{data},\lVert h\rVert_{L^{d}(\mathcal{B})})
and

\beta_{p}:=\frac{2m(p-s_{*})}{s_{*}p-2m[s_{*}+p(\sigma+\hat{\sigma})]}\ \ \mbox{and}\ \ \theta_{p}:=\frac{s_{*}p-2ms_{*}(1+\sigma+\hat{\sigma})}{s_{*}p-2m[s_{*}+p(\sigma+\hat{\sigma})]}\,.

(6.79)

Lemma
2
with the choice
\mathcal{Z}(t)\equiv\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{p}(B_{t})}
now gives (
6.75
).

We finally come to the a priori gradient bound in the two dimensional case.

Proposition 4

Let
u\in W^{1,\gamma}(\mathcal{B};\mathbb{R}^{N})
be a solution to
(
6.1
)
under assumptions
set
in
(
4.20
)
for
n=2
. If
B_{\varsigma}\Subset B_{\varrho}
are concentric balls contained in
\mathcal{B}
, then

\displaystyle[E_{\varepsilon}(\lVert Du\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{\varsigma})})]^{\gamma}+\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{\varsigma})}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{c}{(\varrho-\varsigma)^{\beta}}\left[\|F_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,Du)\|_{L^{1}(B_{\varrho})}^{\theta}+\lVert f\rVert_{L^{2}(\log L)^{\mathfrak{a}}(B_{\varrho})}^{\theta}+1\right]\,,

(6.80)

holds with
c\equiv c(\texttt{data},\lVert h\rVert_{L^{d}(\mathcal{B})})\geq 1
,
\beta,\theta\equiv\beta,\theta(d,\gamma,\sigma,\hat{\sigma})>0
.

Proof

We proceed as for the proof of Proposition
3
, keeping the notation used there, including (
6.65
). By Lemma
8
we use Lemma
1
with the choice made at the end of the proof of Lemma
9
, and this for
n=2
gives

\displaystyle G_{\varepsilon}(x_{0},\lvert Du(x_{0})\rvert)

\displaystyle\quad\leq cr_{0}^{-2/p}\left[\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{\tau_{2}})}^{(1+\delta)\vartheta\sigma/2}+1\right]\|G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\|_{L^{p}(B_{r_{0}}(x_{0}))}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+c\left[\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{\tau_{2}})}^{\delta\vartheta\sigma/2}+1\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad\times\left[\mathbf{P}_{1}^{\mathfrak{h}}(\cdot,2r_{0})+\lVert Du\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{\tau_{2}})}\mathbf{P}_{1}^{f}(\cdot,2r_{0})\right],

(6.81)

for every
\delta\in(0,1/2)
, where
c\equiv c(\texttt{data},\lVert h\rVert_{L^{d}(\mathcal{B})},\delta)
and
\vartheta
is as in (
4.15
).
Then (
6.75
) gives

\displaystyle\|G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\|_{L^{p}(B_{r_{0}}(x_{0}))}

\displaystyle\quad\leq cr_{0}^{-\beta_{p}}\left[\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{1}(B_{2r_{0}})}^{\theta_{p}}+1\right]+c\lVert Du\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{\tau_{2}})}\|f\|_{L^{2}(B_{2r_{0}})}

where
p
is as in (
6.65
) with
n=2
,
c\equiv c(\texttt{data},\lVert h\rVert_{L^{d}(\mathcal{B})},p)
and
\beta_{p},\theta_{p}
are as in (
6.79
). Note that here we are using Lemma
10
with the choice
s_{*}=1
, which is allowed as the assumption gives that
\sigma+\hat{\sigma}<1/2-1/d
, which is (
4.15
) for
n=2
. Combining the last two estimates, that hold for a.e.
x_{0}\in B_{\tau_{1}}
, we have

\displaystyle\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{\tau_{1}})}

\displaystyle\ \ \leq\frac{c}{(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})^{2/p+\beta_{p}}}\left[\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{\tau_{2}})}^{(1+\delta)\vartheta\sigma/2}+1\right]\left[\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{1}(B_{\varrho})}^{\theta_{p}}+1\right]

\displaystyle\ \ \qquad+\frac{c}{(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})^{2/p}}\left[\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{\tau_{2}})}^{(1+\delta)\vartheta\sigma/2}+1\right]\lVert Du\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{\tau_{2}})}\|f\|_{L^{2}(B_{\varrho})}

\displaystyle\ \ \qquad+c\left[\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{\tau_{2}})}^{\delta\vartheta\sigma/2}+1\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt\times\lVert Du\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{\tau_{2}})}\|\mathbf{P}_{1}^{f}(\cdot,(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})/4)\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{\tau_{1}})}

\displaystyle\ \ \qquad+c\left[\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{\tau_{2}})}^{\delta\vartheta\sigma/2}+1\right]\|\mathbf{P}_{1}^{\mathfrak{h}}(\cdot,(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})/4)\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{\tau_{1}})}

\displaystyle\ \ =:T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}+T_{4}\,,

(6.82)

for
c\equiv c(\texttt{data},\lVert h\rVert_{L^{d}(\mathcal{B})},\delta)
. To estimate the
T
-terms we take
\delta
such that

(1+\delta)\vartheta\sigma/2+1/\gamma<1\quad\mbox{and}\quad\delta\vartheta\sigma/2+(1+\sigma+\hat{\sigma})/\gamma<1

(6.83)

hold, which is in turn possible by (
4.15
)
2
; this fixes
\delta
as a function of
\sigma,\tilde{\sigma},\gamma
. We then have

\displaystyle T_{1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{sceglidelta}}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{8}\,\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{\tau_{2}})}+\frac{c\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{1}(B_{\varrho})}^{\theta}+c}{(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})^{\beta}}\,,

\displaystyle T_{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{sos0.1}}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\frac{c}{(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})^{2/p}}[\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{\tau_{2}})}+1]^{(1+\delta)\vartheta\sigma/2+1/\gamma}\|f\|_{L^{2}(B_{\varrho})}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{sceglidelta}}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{8}\,\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{\tau_{2}})}+\frac{c\|f\|_{L^{2}(\log L)^{\mathfrak{a}}(B_{\varrho})}^{\theta}+c}{(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})^{\beta}}\,,

\displaystyle T_{3}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{potlore}_{2},\eqref{sos0.1}}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle c[\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{\tau_{2}})}+1]^{\delta\vartheta\sigma/2+1/\gamma}\|f\|_{L^{2}(\log L)^{\mathfrak{a}}(B_{\varrho})}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{sceglidelta}}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{8}\,\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{\tau_{2}})}+c\|f\|_{L^{2}(\log L)^{\mathfrak{a}}(B_{\varrho})}^{\theta}+1\,,

with
c\equiv c(\texttt{data},\lVert h\rVert_{L^{d}(\mathcal{B})})
. Here, as in the following lines,
\theta,\beta
denote positive exponents depending on
d,\gamma,\sigma,\hat{\sigma}
; they might change from line to line according to the same convention used to denote a generic constant
c
. To estimate the last term
T_{4}
we again use Lemma
10
with
s_{*}=1
. Therefore, we set
\tau_{3}:=(\tau_{1}+\tau_{2})/2
so that
\tau_{2}-\tau_{3}=(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})/2
, and, recalling (
6.65
) for
n=2
, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{P}_{1}^{\mathfrak{h}}(\cdot,(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})/4)\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{\tau_{1}})}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{potlore}_{2}}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle c\lVert\mathfrak{h}\rVert_{L^{2}(\log L)^{\mathfrak{a}}(B_{\tau_{3}})}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{normine})}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle c(\tau)\lVert\mathfrak{h}\rVert_{L^{2+\tau}(B_{\tau_{3}})}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{fk0})}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle c\lVert h\rVert_{L^{d}(B_{\tau_{3}})}\lVert\bar{G}_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{p}(B_{\tau_{3}})}^{1+\sigma+\hat{\sigma}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{2.2})}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\frac{c}{(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})^{\beta_{*}}}\left[\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{1}(B_{\tau_{2}})}^{\theta_{*}}+1\right]

\displaystyle\ \ +c\left[\|Du\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{\tau_{2}})}\|f\|_{L^{2}(B_{\tau_{2}})}\right]^{1+\sigma+\hat{\sigma}}

for
c\equiv c(\texttt{data},\lVert h\rVert_{L^{d}(\mathcal{B})})
. Using the above inequality and (
5.19
)
1
, we end up with

\displaystyle T_{4}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c}{(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})^{\beta_{*}}}\left[\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{\tau_{2}})}^{\delta\vartheta\sigma/2}+1\right]\left[\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{1}(B_{\varrho})}^{\theta_{*}}+1\right]

\displaystyle\quad+c\left[\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{\tau_{2}})}^{\delta\vartheta\sigma/2+(1+\sigma+\hat{\sigma})/\gamma}+1\right]\|f\|_{L^{2}(\log L)^{\mathfrak{a}}(B_{\varrho})}^{1+\sigma+\hat{\sigma}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{8}\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{\tau_{2}})}+\frac{c\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{1}(B_{\varrho})}^{\theta}+c}{(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})^{\beta}}+c\|f\|_{L^{2}(\log L)^{\mathfrak{a}}(B_{\varrho})}^{\theta}

where
c\equiv c(\texttt{data},\lVert h\rVert_{L^{d}(\mathcal{B})})
, and we have used the second condition in (
6.83
). Plugging the estimates for the
T
-terms in (
6.82
) yields

\displaystyle\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{\tau_{1}})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{\tau_{2}})}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{c\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{1}(B_{\varrho})}^{\theta}+c\|f\|_{L^{2}(\log L)^{\mathfrak{a}}(B_{\varrho})}^{\theta}+c}{(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})^{\beta}}

for new exponents
\theta,\beta
as in the statement.
Lemma
2
allows now to conclude with

\displaystyle\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{\varsigma})}\leq\frac{c\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{1}(B_{\varrho})}^{\theta}+c\|f\|_{L^{2}(\log L)^{\mathfrak{a}}(B_{\varrho})}^{\theta}+c}{(\varrho-\varsigma)^{\beta}}\,,

where
c\equiv c(\texttt{data},\lVert h\rVert_{L^{d}(\mathcal{B})})
.
Finally, (
4
) follows from this last estimate, (
5.18
) and (
5.19
)
1
. The proof is complete.

7
Proofs of Theorems
4.1
and
4.3

We start with the proof of Theorem
4.3
, where, in particular, we assume
f\in L^{n}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{N})
and
n>2
. We fix
p
as in statement and, without loss of generality, we assume that
p>1+\sigma
(
\sigma
being as in (
4.15
)). Then, for every integer
j\geq 1
, we define
f_{j}\in L^{\infty}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{N})
as
f_{j}(x):=f(x)
if
\lvert f(x)\rvert\leq j
, and
f_{j}(x):=j|f(x)|^{-1}f(x)
otherwise. It clearly follows that

|f_{j}|\leq|f|\quad\mbox{for every $j\geq 1$}\,,\qquad f_{j}\to f\ \mbox{in $L^{n}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{N})$}\,.

(7.1)

Next, we determine
R_{*}\equiv R_{*}(\texttt{data},f(\cdot),p)\leq 1
according to Proposition
1
. Pay attention here; with some abuse of notation, the
f
used here is not the same from Proposition
1
, but rather corresponds to
\mathfrak{f}
from (
6.1
) in the context of Proposition
1
(and thanks to (
7.1
),
f_{j}
corresponds to
f
in Proposition
1
). Accordingly, we fix a ball
\mathcal{B}\Subset\Omega
such that
\texttt{r}(\mathcal{B})\leq R_{*}
. We consider a decreasing sequence of positive numbers
\{\varepsilon_{j}\}
such that

\varepsilon_{j}\leq\min\{1,T\}/4
for every
j\in\mathbb{N}
, and, accordingly, we consider the families of functions
\{F_{j}\}\equiv\{F_{\varepsilon_{j}}\}
,
\{G_{j}\}\equiv\{G_{\varepsilon_{j}}\}
constructed in (
5.4
). Note now that any minimizer
u
of the functional
\mathcal{F}
in (
1.1
) belongs to
W^{1,\gamma}_{{\rm loc}}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{N})
by (
5.20
)
1
. This allows to define
u_{j}\in u+W^{1,\gamma}_{0}(\mathcal{B};\mathbb{R}^{N})
as the solution to

\displaystyle u_{j}\mapsto\min_{w\in u+W^{1,\gamma}_{0}(\mathcal{B};\mathbb{R}^{N})}\,\mathcal{F}_{j}(w;\mathcal{B})

\displaystyle\qquad:=\min_{w\in u+W^{1,\gamma}_{0}(\mathcal{B};\mathbb{R}^{N})}\,\int_{\mathcal{B}}\left[F_{j}(x,Dw)-f_{j}\cdot w\right]\,dx\,.

Directs Methods of the Calculus of Variations apply here and ensure existence (see for instance
(
BM
, Section 4.4)
).
As for
(
BM
, (4.55))
, and recalling (
5.20
)
2
and (
5.22
), we find

\displaystyle\|F_{j}(\cdot,Du_{j})\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{B})}+\|Du_{j}\|_{L^{\gamma}(\mathcal{B})}^{\gamma}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq c\left[\|F(\cdot,Du)+1\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{B})}+[\texttt{r}(\mathcal{B})]^{n}\lVert f\rVert_{L^{n}(\mathcal{B})}^{\gamma/(\gamma-1)}\right]

(7.2)

for every
j\geq 1
, where
c\equiv c(n,N,\nu,\gamma,T)
. This implies we can assume that, up to a not relabelled subsequence,
Du_{j}\rightharpoonup D\tilde{u}
weakly in
L^{\gamma}(\mathcal{B};\mathbb{R}^{N\times n})
and

u_{j}\to\tilde{u}
strongly in
L^{\frac{n}{n-1}}(\mathcal{B};\mathbb{R}^{N})
, for some
\tilde{u}\in u+W^{1,\gamma}_{0}(\mathcal{B};\mathbb{R}^{N})
. Note that Proposition
1
applies to
u_{j}
with the choice
s_{*}=1
, as (
6.15
) is satisfied assuming that
\sigma+\hat{\sigma}<1/n-1/d
, which is the case here by (
4.15
) in
set
. The application of Proposition
1
, and (
6.16
), now give that

\displaystyle\|G_{j}(\cdot,\lvert Du_{j}\rvert)\|_{L^{p}(s\mathcal{B})}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{c}{(1-s)^{\beta_{p}}[\texttt{r}(\mathcal{B})]^{\beta_{p}}}\left[\|F(\cdot,Du)\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{B})}^{\theta_{p}}+\lVert f\rVert_{L^{n}(\mathcal{B})}^{\theta_{p}}+1\right]

(7.3)

holds for every
s\in(0,1)
, for new exponents
\beta_{p},\theta_{p}\equiv\beta_{p},\theta_{p}(n,\gamma,d,\sigma,\hat{\sigma},p)>0
, where we have also used (
7
) to bound the right-side coming from (
6.16
);
c
depends on
data
and
\lVert h\rVert_{L^{d}(\mathcal{B})}
. We fix
j_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
, and apply (
5.33
) with
j\geq j_{0}
in (
7.3
), to get

\displaystyle\|G_{j_{0}}(\cdot,\lvert Du_{j}\rvert)\|_{L^{p}(s\mathcal{B})}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{c}{(1-s)^{\beta_{p}}[\texttt{r}(\mathcal{B})]^{\beta_{p}}}\left[\|F(\cdot,Du)\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{B})}^{\theta_{p}}+\lVert f\rVert_{L^{n}(\mathcal{B})}^{\theta_{p}}+1\right]\,.

(7.4)

Letting
j\to\infty
in the above inequality, and using weak lower semicontinuity (recall that
G_{j_{0}}(\cdot)
is convex and non-decreasing, by (
5.17
),
\varepsilon\equiv\varepsilon_{j_{0}}
, therefore
z\mapsto G_{j_{0}}(\cdot,\lvert z\rvert)
is convex), yields

\displaystyle\|G_{j_{0}}(\cdot,\lvert D\tilde{u}\rvert)\|_{L^{p}(s\mathcal{B})}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{c}{(1-s)^{\beta_{p}}[\texttt{r}(\mathcal{B})]^{\beta_{p}}}\left[\|F(\cdot,Du)\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{B})}^{\theta_{p}}+\lVert f\rVert_{L^{n}(\mathcal{B})}^{\theta_{p}}+1\right]\,.

(7.5)

This holds for every
j_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
and therefore, by finally letting

j_{0}\to\infty
(by Fatou’s lemma) and recalling (
5.33
), we conclude with

\displaystyle\|G(\cdot,\lvert D\tilde{u}\rvert)\|_{L^{p}(s\mathcal{B})}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{c}{(1-s)^{\beta_{p}}[\texttt{r}(\mathcal{B})]^{\beta_{p}}}\left[\|F(\cdot,Du)\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{B})}^{\theta_{p}}+\lVert f\rVert_{L^{n}(\mathcal{B})}^{\theta_{p}}+1\right]\,,

(7.6)

for every
s\in(0,1)
, where
c\equiv c(\texttt{data},\lVert h\rVert_{L^{d}(\mathcal{B})})
and
\beta_{p},\theta_{p}\equiv\beta_{p},\theta_{p}(n,\gamma,d,\sigma,\linebreak\hat{\sigma},p)>0
.
Next, we trivially write

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{j_{0}}(u_{j};s\mathcal{B})

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{F}_{j}(u_{j};s\mathcal{B})+\|F_{j}(\cdot,Du_{j})-F_{j_{0}}(\cdot,Du_{j})\|_{L^{1}(s\mathcal{B})}

\displaystyle\quad+\|(f_{j_{0}}-f_{j})\cdot u_{j}\|_{L^{1}(s\mathcal{B})}

(7.7)

whenever
s\in(0,1)
.
Properties (
7.1
)-(
7
), Hölder and Sobolev-Poincaré inequalities give

\mathcal{F}_{j}(u_{j};s\mathcal{B})\leq\mathcal{F}_{j}(u_{j};\mathcal{B})+\|{f_{j}\cdot u_{j}\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{B}\setminus s\mathcal{B})}}\leq\mathcal{F}_{j}(u;\mathcal{B})+c\lVert f\rVert_{L^{n}(\mathcal{B}\setminus s\mathcal{B})},

(7.8)

where
c
is independent of
s,j
and we have used minimality of
u_{j}
. Using (
5.42
) with
\varepsilon_{1}\equiv\varepsilon_{j}
,
\varepsilon_{2}\equiv\varepsilon_{j_{0}}
,
w\equiv u_{j}
,
B\equiv s\mathcal{B}
(recall it is
p>1+\sigma
) and (
7.4
), we have

\|F_{j}(\cdot,Du_{j})-F_{j_{0}}(\cdot,Du_{j})\|_{L^{1}(s\mathcal{B})}\leq c(1-s)^{-\beta_{p}}\texttt{o}(j_{0})

(7.9)

where
c
is independent of
s,j,j_{0}
. Again Sobolev-Poincaré inequality (
7.1
) and (
7
) give

\|(f_{j_{0}}-f_{j})\cdot u_{j}\|_{L^{1}(s\mathcal{B})}\leq c\|f_{j_{0}}-f_{j}\|_{L^{n}(s\mathcal{B})}
, with
c
independent of
s,j,j_{0}
. Using this last inequality and (
7.8
)-(
7.9
) in (
7.7
), and finally letting
j\to\infty
, lower semicontinuity yields

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{j_{0}}(\tilde{u};s\mathcal{B})

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{F}(u;\mathcal{B})+c\lVert f\rVert_{L^{n}(\mathcal{B}\setminus s\mathcal{B})}

\displaystyle\quad+c\lVert f_{j_{0}}-f\rVert_{L^{n}(\mathcal{B})}+c(1-s)^{-\beta_{p}}\texttt{o}(j_{0})\,.

(7.10)

Note that we have used (
5.22
) and Lebesgue’s dominated convergence theorem to get
\mathcal{F}_{j}(u;\mathcal{B})\to\mathcal{F}(u;\mathcal{B})
. In turn, again note that (
7.6
) ensures that
G(\cdot,\lvert D\tilde{u}\rvert)\in L^{p}(s\mathcal{B})
and therefore allows to apply (
5.43
); this yields

\|F(\cdot,D\tilde{u})-F_{j_{0}}(\cdot,D\tilde{u})\|_{L^{1}(s\mathcal{B})}\leq c(1-s)^{-\beta_{p}}\texttt{o}(j_{0}),

so that
\mathcal{F}_{j_{0}}(\tilde{u};s\mathcal{B})\to\mathcal{F}(\tilde{u};s\mathcal{B})
as
j_{0}\to\infty
, where we also use (
7.1
).
In view of this, letting first
j_{0}\to\infty
and then
s\to 1
in (
7.10
), yields

\mathcal{F}(\tilde{u};\mathcal{B})\leq\mathcal{F}(u;\mathcal{B})
. This and the minimality of
u
finally give
\mathcal{F}(u;\mathcal{B})=\mathcal{F}(\tilde{u};B)
, therefore, by standard convexity arguments, see for instance
(
BM
, Section 4.4)
, we end up with

\displaystyle\mbox{either}\ \max\{\lvert Du(x)\rvert,\lvert D\tilde{u}(x)\rvert\}\leq T\ \ \mbox{or}\ \ Du(x)=D\tilde{u}(x)

(7.11)

for a.e.
x\in\mathcal{B}
.
Using this information in (
7.6
) yields (
4.24
) and concludes the proof of Theorem
4.3
. Observe that, in order to justify the content of Remark
4
it is sufficient to note that making the a priori estimate (
7.3
) only requires the bound
\sigma+\hat{\sigma}<1/n-1/d
.

We now come to the proof of Theorem
4.1
. We can again use the same approximation employed for Theorem
4.3
. Using this time estimate (
3
) for the case
n>2
and estimate (
4
) when
n=2
, together with (
7
), we find

\displaystyle[E_{\varepsilon_{j}}(\lVert Du_{j}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(s\mathcal{B})})]^{\gamma}+\lVert G_{j}(\cdot,\lvert Du_{j}\rvert)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(s\mathcal{B})}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\leq\frac{c}{(1-s)^{\beta}[\texttt{r}(\mathcal{B})]^{\beta}}\left[\|F(\cdot,Du)\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{B})}^{\theta}+\lVert f\rVert_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathcal{B})}^{\theta}+1\right]

(7.12)

for every
s\in(0,1)
, where
c,\beta,\theta
have the same dependencies as in (
3
).
It follows that for every
s\in(0,1)
there exists
M_{s}
such that
\|Du_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}(s\mathcal{B})}\leq M_{s}
, for every
j\in\mathbb{N}
. Using a standard diagonalization argument we infer that, up to a not relabelled subsequences, we have that
u_{j}\rightharpoonup^{*}\tilde{u}
in
W^{1,\infty}_{{\rm loc}}(\mathcal{B};\mathbb{R}^{N})
for some
\tilde{u}\in u+W^{1,\gamma}_{0}(\mathcal{B};\mathbb{R}^{N})
being such that
\|D\tilde{u}\|_{L^{\infty}(s\mathcal{B})}\leq M_{s}
. Moreover, we can repeat verbatim the argument of Theorem
4.3
leading to (
7.11
). Now, denoting
j_{s}
the first integer such that
1/\varepsilon_{j_{s}}>M_{s}
, from the very definition of
G_{j}\equiv G_{\varepsilon_{j}}
in (
5.11
), it follows that
\lVert G_{j}(\cdot,\lvert Du_{j}\rvert)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(s\mathcal{B})}=\mathfrak{g}_{1}\lVert G(\cdot,\lvert Du_{j}\rvert)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(s\mathcal{B})}
,
for every
j\geq j_{s}
. Therefore, letting
j\to\infty
in (
7
), and using (
7.11
), yields (
4.22
) and the proof of Theorem
4.1
is complete too. Notice that here we are using standard lower semicontinuity theorems for supremal functionals with respect to the weak
∗
convergence in
W^{1,\infty}_{{\rm loc}}(\mathcal{B};\mathbb{R}^{N})
(see for instance
BJW
).

Remark 9

Let us discuss the case
f\equiv 0
.
We start by the case
n>2
; the first relation in (
6.70
) is already implied by
\sigma+\hat{\sigma}<1/n-1/d
. Moreover, note that the second condition in (
6.70
) appears only when
f\not\equiv 0
. This is the only point in the proof of Theorem
4.1
where the full bound in (
4.15
)
1
is required; otherwise

\sigma+\hat{\sigma}<1/n-1/d
is sufficient. Alternatively, one can apply directly Proposition
2
instead of Proposition
1
in the proof of Theorem
4.1
. When
n=2
and
f\equiv 0
, (
6.83
) turns into
(1+\delta)\vartheta\sigma/2<1
and
\delta\vartheta\sigma/2<1
, that are implied by
\sigma+\hat{\sigma}<1/2-1/d
by taking
\delta
small enough (see estimate of
T_{4}
in Proposition
4
). Finally, note that in the case
\gamma\geq 2
, the minimum in (
4.15
) is attained by
1/n-1/d
. All in all, we have justified the content of Remark
3
.

Lemma 11

Under assumptions
\texttt{set}_{\textnormal{u}}
in
(
4.20
)

•

The following inequalities hold for every
t\in[T,\infty)
:

\begin{cases}\displaystyle\ g_{1,\varepsilon}(t)\leq g_{2,\varepsilon}(t)\leq\tilde{c}_{\textnormal{u}}g_{1,\varepsilon}(t)\\
g_{3,\varepsilon}(t)\leq\tilde{c}_{\textnormal{u}}g_{1,\varepsilon}(t)t\end{cases}

(8.1)

where
\tilde{c}_{\textnormal{u}}\equiv\tilde{c}_{\textnormal{u}}(n,N,\nu,\gamma,c_{\textnormal{u}})\geq 1
, and

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\tilde{c}_{\textnormal{u}}}g_{1,\varepsilon}(t)t^{2}\leq G_{\varepsilon}(t)+\frac{\mathfrak{g}_{1}}{2}g_{1}(T)T^{2}\leq\frac{\tilde{c}_{\textnormal{u}}}{2}g_{1,\varepsilon}(t)t^{2}\,.

(8.2)

•

Moreover, for every
x\in\Omega
and
t\geq 0
, it holds that

G_{\varepsilon}(t)\leq\tilde{F}_{\varepsilon}(x,t)\leq cG_{\varepsilon}(t)+cg_{1}(T)(T^{2}+\mu_{\varepsilon}^{2})\,,\quad c\equiv c(\texttt{data}_{\textnormal{u}})\,.

(8.3)

Proof

Properties (
8.1
) are immediate and follow from (
4.19
), also recalling (
4.6
), therefore we concentrate on (
8.2
).
As for the right-hand side of (
8.2
), integration by parts yields

\int_{T}^{t}\left[\tilde{a}_{\varepsilon}(x,s)+\tilde{a}_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(x,s)s\right]s\,ds=-\int_{T}^{t}\tilde{a}_{\varepsilon}(x,s)s\,ds+\tilde{a}_{\varepsilon}(x,t)t^{2}-\tilde{a}_{\varepsilon}(x,T)T^{2}

(8.4)

and therefore, recalling the definition in (
5.7
), we have

\displaystyle G_{\varepsilon}(t)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{a2}_{2}}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\int_{T}^{t}\left[\tilde{a}_{\varepsilon}(x,s)+\tilde{a}_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(x,s)s\right]s\,ds

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{a1}),(\ref{uni3})}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle-G_{\varepsilon}(t)+\tilde{c}_{\textnormal{u}}g_{1,\varepsilon}(t)t^{2}-\mathfrak{g}_{1}g_{1}(T)T^{2}

that is, the right-hand side of (
8.2
). As for the left-hand side, we similarly have

\displaystyle G_{\varepsilon}(t)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{a2})_{2},(\ref{uni3})}}{{\geq}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\tilde{c}_{\textnormal{u}}}\int_{T}^{t}\left[\tilde{a}_{\varepsilon}(x,s)+\tilde{a}_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(x,s)s\right]s\,ds

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{a1}),(\ref{intpart})}}{{\geq}}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\tilde{c_{\textnormal{u}}}}\int_{T}^{t}g_{2,\varepsilon}(s)s\,ds+\frac{1}{\tilde{c_{\textnormal{u}}}}\left[\tilde{a}_{\varepsilon}(x,t)t^{2}-\tilde{a}_{\varepsilon}(x,T)T^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{g1e},(\ref{uni3})}}{{\geq}}

\displaystyle-G_{\varepsilon}(t)+\frac{1}{\tilde{c_{\textnormal{u}}}}g_{1,\varepsilon}(t)t^{2}-\mathfrak{g}_{1}g_{1}(T)T^{2}\,.

We turn to (
8.3
). The left-hand side inequality is nothing but (
5.18
)
3
(that holds whenever
|z|\geq 0
). For the right-hand side inequality, when
t\leq T
we have, similarly to (
5.28
)

\tilde{F}_{\varepsilon}(x,t)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{uni0}_{2},(\ref{slipffff})}}{{\leq}}c\left[\frac{g_{1}(T)}{(T^{2}+\mu^{2})^{\frac{\gamma-2}{2}}}+\varepsilon\right](T^{2}+\mu_{\varepsilon}^{2})^{\gamma/2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{00bis})}}{{\leq}}cg_{1}(T)(T^{2}+\mu^{2}).

When
t>T
, we note that (
5.15
) and (
8.1
) imply
\bar{a}_{\varepsilon}(x,s)\leq cg_{1,\varepsilon}(s)
, that implies
\tilde{F}_{\varepsilon}(x,t)-\tilde{F}_{\varepsilon}(x,T)\leq cG_{\varepsilon}(t)
, from which (
8.3
) follows again using the content of the last display.

Proposition 5

Let
u\in W^{1,\gamma}(\mathcal{B};\mathbb{R}^{N})
be a solution to
(
6.1
)
, under assumptions
\texttt{set}_{\textnormal{u}}
in
(
4.20
)
for
n\geq 2
. There exists a positive radius
R_{*}\equiv R_{*}(\texttt{data}_{\textnormal{u}},h(\cdot))\leq 1
such that if
\texttt{r}(\mathcal{B})\leq R_{*}
and
B_{\varsigma}\Subset B_{\varrho}
are concentric balls contained in
\mathcal{B}
, then

\displaystyle\|F_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,Du)\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{\xi})}+\|G_{\varepsilon}(\lvert Du\rvert)\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{\xi})}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\leq\frac{c}{(\varrho-\xi)^{n}}\|F_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,Du)\|_{L^{1}(B_{\varrho})}+c\left[\|f\|_{\mathfrak{X}(B_{\varrho})}^{\gamma/(\gamma-1)}+1\right]

(8.5)

holds with
c\equiv c(\texttt{data}_{\textnormal{u}})
, where
\mathfrak{X}(\cdot)
has been defined in (
1.9
).

Proof

We start taking a ball
B_{r}(x_{0})\Subset\mathcal{B}
(therefore it is
r<1
), and prove that

\displaystyle\int_{B_{r/2}(x_{0})}\lvert D(G_{\varepsilon}(\lvert Du\rvert)-\kappa)_{+}\rvert^{2}\,dx\leq\frac{c}{r^{2}}\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}(G_{\varepsilon}(\lvert Du\rvert)-\kappa)_{+}^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\hskip 71.13188pt+c\left[\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{r}(x_{0}))}+1\right]^{2}\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}|h|^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt+c\lVert Du\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{r}(x_{0}))}^{2}\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}\lvert f\rvert^{2}\,dx

(8.6)

holds whenever
\kappa\geq 0
, with
c\equiv c(\texttt{data}_{\textnormal{u}})
. This is an analogue of (
8
) and to get it we modify the proof of Lemma
8
, keeping the notation used there. Proceeding as for the bounds for
\mbox{(IV)}_{z}
-
\mbox{(VI)}_{z}
in Lemma
8
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{3}+\mathcal{S}_{4}

\displaystyle\leq c|\mbox{(IV)}_{x}|+c|\mbox{(V)}_{x}|+c|\mbox{(VI)}_{x}|+\frac{c}{r^{2}}\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}(G_{\varepsilon}(\lvert Du\rvert)-\kappa)_{+}^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\qquad+c\sum_{s=1}^{n}\int_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}}\lvert f\cdot D_{s}\varphi_{s}\rvert\,dx\,,

(8.7)

with
c\equiv c(\texttt{data}_{\textnormal{u}})
. This estimate can be obtained by adapting those in (
6.49
)-(
6.51
), and also those for the terms in (
6.20
). One must take into account that now
g_{1,\varepsilon}
is independent of
x
(therefore the terms coming from the use of (
5.31
) and featuring
h(\cdot)
disappear), and the fact that we can formally take
\sigma\equiv 0
as the ratio
g_{2,\varepsilon}/g_{1,\varepsilon}
is bounded by a constant by (
8.1
)
1
. In turn, the last term in (
8.7
) involving the right-hand side
f
can be treated exactly as in (
6.58
). As for the remaining
x
-terms appearing in the first line of (
8.7
), with the help of
\eqref{growthd}_{3}
, (
8.1
) and (
8.2
), we estimate

\displaystyle c|\mbox{(IV)}_{x}|+c|\mbox{(V)}_{x}|+c|\mbox{(VI)}_{x}|

\displaystyle\leq\bar{\varepsilon}\mathcal{S}_{3}+\bar{\varepsilon}\mathcal{S}_{4}+\frac{c}{r^{2}}\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}(G_{\varepsilon}(\lvert Du\rvert)-\kappa)_{+}^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{c}{\bar{\varepsilon}}\int_{\mathcal{B}}\eta^{2}|h|^{2}[G_{\varepsilon}(\lvert Du\rvert)+1]^{2}\,dx\,,

for
c\equiv c(\texttt{data}_{\textnormal{u}})
and
\bar{\varepsilon}\in(0,1)
. Merging the content of the above two displays, choosing
\bar{\varepsilon}\equiv\bar{\varepsilon}(\texttt{data}_{\textnormal{u}})
small enough, and reabsorbing terms, we end up with (
8.6
),
where
c\equiv c(\texttt{data}_{\textnormal{u}})
. As a consequence, we proceed as for the proofs of Lemma

9
and Proposition
3
. An application of Lemma
1
gives that, if
B_{2r_{0}}(x_{0})\Subset\mathcal{B}
is any ball, then

\displaystyle G_{\varepsilon}(\lvert Du(x_{0})\rvert)

\displaystyle\leq cr_{0}^{-n/2}\left[\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{\tau_{2}})}+1\right]^{1/2}\|G_{\varepsilon}(\lvert Du\rvert)\|_{L^{1}(B_{\tau_{2}})}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\quad+c\left[\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{\tau_{2}})}+1\right]\mathbf{P}_{1}^{h}(x_{0},2r_{0})

\displaystyle\quad+c\left[\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{\tau_{2}})}+1\right]^{1/\gamma}\mathbf{P}_{1}^{f}(x_{0},2r_{0})

(8.8)

holds provided
x_{0}
is a Lebesgue point of
|Du|
, where
c\equiv c(\texttt{data}_{\textnormal{u}})
, and we have also used (
5.19
)
1
. Next, (
3.1
) gives that

\|\mathbf{P}_{1}^{h}(\cdot,2r_{0})\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{\tau_{1}})}\lesssim\|h\|_{\mathfrak{X}(B_{\varrho})}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\|\mathbf{P}_{1}^{f}(\cdot,2r_{0})\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{\tau_{1}})}\lesssim\|f\|_{\mathfrak{X}(B_{\varrho})}\,.

Using these informations in (
8.8
) yields

\displaystyle\|G_{\varepsilon}(\lvert Du\rvert)\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{\tau_{1}})}+1

\displaystyle\qquad\leq cr_{0}^{-n/2}\left[\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{\tau_{2}})}+1\right]^{1/2}\|G_{\varepsilon}(\lvert Du\rvert)\|_{L^{1}(B_{\tau_{2}})}^{1/2}+1

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+c_{*}\left[\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{\tau_{2}})}+1\right]\|h\|_{\mathfrak{X}(B_{\varrho})}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+c\left[\lVert G_{\varepsilon}(\lvert Du\rvert)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{\tau_{2}})}+1\right]^{1/\gamma}\|f\|_{\mathfrak{X}(B_{\varrho})}\,,

(8.9)

where
c,c_{*}\equiv c,c_{*}(\texttt{data}_{\textnormal{u}})
. By absolute continuity, we now determine the radius
R_{*}\equiv R_{*}(\texttt{data}_{\textnormal{u}},h(\cdot))
mentioned in the statement in such a way that

\displaystyle\texttt{r}(\mathcal{B})\leq R_{*}\Longrightarrow c_{*}\lVert h\rVert_{\mathfrak{X}(B_{\varrho})}\leq c_{*}\lVert h\rVert_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathcal{B})}\leq 1/6\,.

(8.10)

Using this and Young inequality in (
Proof
), and yet recalling that
r_{0}:=(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})/8
, gives

\displaystyle\|G_{\varepsilon}(\lvert Du\rvert)\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{\tau_{1}})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\|G_{\varepsilon}(\lvert Du\rvert)\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{\tau_{2}})}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{c}{(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})^{n}}\|G_{\varepsilon}(\lvert Du\rvert)\|_{L^{1}(B_{\varrho})}+c\left[\|f\|_{\mathfrak{X}(B_{\varrho})}^{\gamma/(\gamma-1)}+1\right]\,.

Inequality (
8.5
) now follows using Lemma
2
with
\mathcal{Z}(t):=\|G_{\varepsilon}(\lvert Du\rvert)\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{t})}
and (
8.3
).

With Proposition
5
available, we can now complete the proof of Theorem
4.2
. Arguing as in the proof of Theorem
4.1
in Section
7
(as usual with
F_{j}\equiv F_{\varepsilon_{j}}
and so on), and arrive up to (
7
), the analog of which in the present context is

\|F_{j}(\cdot,Du_{j})\|_{L^{\infty}(s\mathcal{B})}\leq\frac{c}{(1-s)^{n}[\texttt{r}(\mathcal{B})]^{n}}\|F(\cdot,Du)\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{B})}+c\left[\|f\|_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathcal{B})}^{\gamma/(\gamma-1)}+1\right]

where

depends on
\texttt{data}_{\textnormal{u}}
(again recall the equivalence in (
